Title: Output-Aware Block Influence: Jacobian-Lens Weighting for Depth Pruning

  • Authors: Kyle Lemoi, Jonathan Yong, Shubhankar Tripathy, Priestley Fernandes, Anirban Majumder, Zarreen Reza
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00015
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00015
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00015]] Output-Aware Block Influence: Jacobian-Lens Weighting for Depth Pruning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00015)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Depth pruning removes entire transformer blocks and therefore depends on a reliable criterion for identifying which blocks can be removed. Block Influence (BI) ranks blocks by how minimally they rotate the hidden state. However, local redundancy does not necessarily imply downstream irrelevance. We address this limitation using the Jacobian Lens (J-Lens) neural artifact, a map transporting perturbations at a layer to the final residual stream. At a common sparsity level, pruning scores applying J-Lens transportation to $BI$, what we call $J-BI$, were found to improve post-pruning accuracy by up to 19 points over $BI$ while reducing damage to perplexity for Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B-IT, and Gemma-3-12B-IT. We also perform a single-block ablation study and use J-Lens probing to explain why the proposed scores improved performance.

Title: Beyond the Last Layer: Global Inter-Layer Aggregation for AI-Generated Image Detection

  • Authors: Wenbin Shen, Zhichao Lian
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00017
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00017
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00017]] Beyond the Last Layer: Global Inter-Layer Aggregation for AI-Generated Image Detection(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00017)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: The rapid development of generative models has made AI-generated images increasingly close to real images. It has also brought several challenges to detectors, including rapid generator updates, large changes in data distribution, and limited cross-dataset generalization. Many existing methods based on pretrained vision encoders primarily use the global representation from the last layer, potentially leaving useful information from intermediate layers underexplored. To address this issue, we propose Global Inter-Layer Aggregation (GILA). GILA extracts global representations from different depths of a frozen encoder, models interactions among layer representations through inter-layer self-attention, and adaptively aggregates the resulting contextualized representations. The method is not tied to a single pretraining objective and does not require fine-tuning the pretrained encoder. We evaluated GILA on three vision backbones with different pretraining objectives, ten seen generators, and three external datasets. The experimental results show that GILA makes better use of multi-layer information in pretrained encoders and improves detection performance and cross-dataset generalization.

Title: SFA: A Statistical Physics-Inspired Diffusion Transformer for Efficient and High-Quality Image Generation

  • Authors: Zixuan Huang (1 and 2), Tianhao You (1), Guozhang Chen (1, 3 and 4) ((1) School of Computer Science, Peking University, Beijing, China, (2) School of Physics, Peking University, Beijing, China, (3) State Key Laboratory of Multimedia Information Processing, Peking University, Beijing, China, (4) National Engineering Research Center of Visual Technology, Peking University, Beijing, China)
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.GR, eess.IV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00027
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00027
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00027]] SFA: A Statistical Physics-Inspired Diffusion Transformer for Efficient and High-Quality Image Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00027)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Diffusion transformers have achieved remarkable success in image generation, but accelerating attention while preserving generation quality remains a major challenge at high resolutions. Inspired by statistical physics and the widespread occurrence of scale-free laws in nature, we introduce Scale-Free Attention (SFA), which preserves fine local interactions while summarizing distant regions at progressively coarser scales. For $N$ tokens, mean pooling, token-count correction, and a shared softmax over a scale-free spatial hierarchy give SFA $O(N\log N)$ complexity in both forward and backward passes. Our Triton implementation achieves $357.3\times$ forward and $265.6\times$ backward attention speedups over FlashAttention at 262,144 tokens. After 40 epochs of PixelFlow training from scratch on ImageNet at $256\times256$ resolution, SFA achieves the best generation quality among five attention mechanisms, including full attention. Fréchet inception distance evaluated on 50,000 generated samples (FID-50k) improves from 13.25 for full attention to 12.51, and the advantage over full attention persists through 80 epochs. SFA also achieves the best generation quality among the compared efficient-attention methods on Flickr-Faces-HQ (FFHQ). Beyond training from scratch, Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium with attention replaced by SFA retains the generation quality of its full-attention counterpart under matched fine-tuning budgets. We obtain similar results when fine-tuning pretrained PixelFlow on ImageNet. SFA establishes scale-free structure as an effective design principle for efficient, high-quality diffusion transformers.

Title: Monocular Badminton Speed Estimation with Air Drag and Court Geometry

  • Authors: Diwen Huang, Sarvesh Madullapalli, Ishan Pendyala, Oliver Chen, Kevin Li, William Wang
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.MM
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00031
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00031
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00031]] Monocular Badminton Speed Estimation with Air Drag and Court Geometry(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00031)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: A single camera cannot distinguish between a slowly moving object nearby and a rapidly moving object in the distance without additional information. For a shuttlecock, air drag provides a strong deceleration cue that can be combined with known court geometry. We fit a gravity-and-drag trajectory to smartphone video and compare a free monocular fit with a fit constrained to cross the net plane. Camera calibration uses court lines, and the trajectory is fitted to observed shuttle positions and frame timestamps by robust nonlinear least squares. We evaluated 51 badminton shots from two recording sessions, spanning drop shots, drives, half-smashes and smashes. The net-plane fit reduced the median absolute speed difference from a drag-based stereo reference from 13.3% to 8.6%. A second reference, obtained by differentiating triangulated positions without a drag model, also gave a smaller median difference for the net-plane fit. The results support combining court geometry with flight dynamics for badminton speed estimation from a single camera.

Title: A Trustworthy Clinical Decision Support Framework for Brain Tumor MRI Classification: Integrating Attention-Guided Learning, Explainable AI, and Out-of-Distribution Validation

  • Authors: Arjun Kumar Gupta, Harminder Pal Monga
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00034
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00034
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00034]] A Trustworthy Clinical Decision Support Framework for Brain Tumor MRI Classification: Integrating Attention-Guided Learning, Explainable AI, and Out-of-Distribution Validation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00034)
  • Keywords: explainability
  • Abstract: Deep learning models for brain tumor diagnosis need to do more than achieve high classification accuracy. For practical use, they should also provide understandable predictions, recognize unsuitable inputs, and work within a usable clinical system. This paper presents a framework for classifying brain MRI into four categories: glioma, meningioma, pituitary tumor, and no tumor. The model uses InceptionResNetV2 with a Convolutional Block Attention Module (CBAM), while Grad-CAM provides visual explanations of its predictions. A two-stage out-of-distribution (OOD) mechanism screens unsuitable inputs using an initial grayscale check followed by Mahalanobis distance, softmax confidence, and predictive entropy. These components are brought together in NeuroScan AI, a real-time web application. Evaluated on a Mendeley brain tumor MRI dataset of 12,064 images, the framework achieved 98.70% validation accuracy, 96.73% test accuracy, and a macro-average AUC of 0.9965. The OOD pipeline achieved 99% detection accuracy on a preliminary 100-image benchmark. Grad-CAM visualizations showed qualitative correspondence with clinically established tumor locations across all four classes. Overall, the work brings classification, explainability, input validation, and deployment into one framework, while recognizing that broader clinical and OOD validation is still required.

Title: Selective Positional Allocation for Noise-Robust Query Retrieval in Small-Object Detection

  • Authors: Yangchen Zeng
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00035
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00035
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00035]] Selective Positional Allocation for Noise-Robust Query Retrieval in Small-Object Detection(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00035)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: In aerial and remote-sensing imagery, detecting tiny targets is frequently hampered less by the representational capacity of the backbone network than by the manner in which positional priors are distributed across ill-defined foreground and background regions. We introduce SPAR (Selective Positional Allocation and Retrieval), a detection framework that re-conceptualizes positional cues as an allocatable resource: they are intensified at semantically meaningful locations while responses driven by scene clutter are actively attenuated. At the heart of SPAR lies Selective Positional Embedding (SPE), an interpretable embedding mechanism paired with an activation-bar visualization that facilitates direct inspection and hyper-parameter tuning. The encoder branch, termed MOHFE, fuses heatmap-modulated positional signals with category-specific and box-level evidence to yield more compact multi-scale features; the decoder branch, termed HIDQ, employs a foreground gating operation to discard background-dominated candidates prior to query decoding. To cope with the extreme sparsity of small-object evidence, we additionally design DPSConv (Dual-Path Sampling Convolution), which augments retrieval-oriented representations through two geometrically complementary sampling routes. Notably, all heatmap supervision is derived from existing detection objectives and consumed exclusively during training, requiring no auxiliary heatmap annotations and leaving inference as an ordinary forward pass with no additional gradient overhead. Comprehensive evaluations on DOTA, DIOR, VisDrone, PASCAL VOC, NWPU VHR-10, and AITOD demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed design: relative to the baseline, SPAR attains an improvement of up to 9.1 percentage points, shortens the decoder from eight layers to three, and reduces model parameters by 59.4% (66.3M versus 163M).

Title: Out-of-distribution AI-generated image detection with zero-phase deconvolution for residual whitening

  • Authors: Yusuf Yigit Pilavcı (UNICAEN, GREYC), Antoine Mallet (CRIStAL), Vincent Itier (IMT Nord Europe, CRIStAL, CERI SN - IMT Nord Europe), Jan Butora (CRIStAL), Jérémie Boulanger (CRIStAL), Patrick Bas (CRIStAL)
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI, eess.IV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00043
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00043
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00043]] Out-of-distribution AI-generated image detection with zero-phase deconvolution for residual whitening(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00043)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Current AI-generated image detection methods struggle in out-of-distribution testing conditions because detectors are usually not robust when evaluated on generators not included in the training set. This work explores a way to mitigate the forensic bias between sources of images -whether real or generated -by removing generator-specific fingerprints. This new training process can be approximated by pre-processing involving a deconvolution operation designed to remove correlated noise patterns inducing loss of generalization. Indeed, we show that whitening the spatial distribution of the image noise residuals can significantly improve the performance of detectors on images coming from generators unseen during training. In particular, an end-to-end CNN model trained on whitened image residuals improves detection by 12.6% on average compared to a training involving original images.

Title: SparseWAM: Driving World-Action Models are Worth 16 Register Tokens

  • Authors: Yixiang Yang, Lizhong Zhang, Xingyu Zhang, Zhiwen Chen, Jie Ling, Qiankun Yu, Xingliang Liu, Yang Lei, Zhipeng Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00044
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00044
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00044]] SparseWAM: Driving World-Action Models are Worth 16 Register Tokens(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00044)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: World Action Models (WAMs) transfer scene-dynamics priors to planning by jointly forecasting future scenes and ego trajectories. However, WAMs built on video generators must generate dense future videos at inference, incurring prohibitive latency, while efficient variants discard the world branch at deployment and thus forgo test-time imagination. Even when dense video latents are replaced with patch-level DINO or JEPA features, these representations still involve long token sequences and encode decision-irrelevant information. We therefore propose SparseWAM, which forecasts in a sparse register space of only 16 tokens per frame, compressed from frozen DINOv2 features and shaped by joint planning-and-scoring pretraining to concentrate decision-relevant information. Within this space, SparseWAM employs a modality-decoupled Mixture-of-Transformers with a causal attention mask that lets action tokens condition on imagined future registers, retaining explicit imagine-then-act reasoning at deployment. Its compactness further enables low-cost multimodal sampling. As a result, our SparseWAM achieves 92.6 PDMS on NAVSIM v1, 90.1 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2, and 39.6 on NavHard at only 164 ms latency, roughly 1/5 that of DriveVA, demonstrating the efficiency of the sparse register space. Code will be released upon acceptance.

Title: Auditing GeoFM Evaluation for Field-Extent Segmentation: Label Proxies, Baselines, and When Frozen Features Match Fine-tuning

  • Authors: Santosh Pant
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00054
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00054
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00054]] Auditing GeoFM Evaluation for Field-Extent Segmentation: Label Proxies, Baselines, and When Frozen Features Match Fine-tuning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00054)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Geospatial foundation-model (GeoFM) benchmarks for agricultural field-extent segmentation often rely on cropland land-cover proxies and compare against per-pixel spectral baselines. We measure how these choices affect conclusions using identical single-date Sentinel-2 pixels from six countries. Replacing polygon-derived field labels with ESA WorldCover cropland raises random-forest AUROC from 0.55-0.82 to 0.79-0.96, showing that the proxy creates a more spectrally separable task. On the actual field-extent labels, a from-scratch U-Net reaches 0.89-0.98 AUROC and has higher point AUROC than a Prithvi frozen linear probe in five of six regions. We then compare a frozen backbone and full fine-tuning using the same decoder for Prithvi and TerraMind, with ten matched seeds. At $\varepsilon=0.02$ AUROC, 9 of 12 region$\times$model cells are TOST-equivalent. Kenya is the common failure case: positives cover roughly 0.1% of pixels, and full fine-tuning performs better for both models. Cross-region results are less favorable to frozen models. On AUROC and average precision (AP), the U-Net leads the full 30-transfer matrix, while an exploratory analysis excluding Kenya finds that both frozen decoders outperform their fine-tuned counterparts; no AUROC difference is detected between frozen TerraMind and the U-Net in that subset. For Prithvi, a frozen decoder trains about $110\times$ fewer parameters than full fine-tuning. These results argue for auditing labels and baselines before interpreting GeoFM rankings, and for testing a frozen decoder before assuming that full fine-tuning will help. Our conclusions are limited to single-date, per-pixel field-extent segmentation, not parcel-boundary or instance delineation.

Title: From Clips to Streams: A Unified Framework for Streaming Sign Language Translation

  • Authors: Hoang Quan Dang, Xin Shen, Yanbin Liu, Xin Yu, Ling Chen
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00055
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00055
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00055]] From Clips to Streams: A Unified Framework for Streaming Sign Language Translation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00055)
  • Keywords: robust, transformer, segmentation
  • Abstract: Sign Language Translation (SLT) research has typically focused on pre-segmented clips, a constraint that disconnects models from the continuous reality of real-world communication. To bridge the gap from clips to streams, we formally define Streaming SLT, a realistic task demanding the joint discovery and translation of linguistic events from continuous, untrimmed video inputs. To address this challenge, we propose StreamSLST, the first end-to-end framework for Streaming Sign Language Segmentation and Translation via Deformable Transformer architecture. Unlike cascaded approaches that suffer from error propagation, StreamSLST employs a parallel decoding strategy to jointly optimize temporal segmentation and translation in a single pass. To enable scalable training on long-form videos, we incorporate a Sentence-aware Sliding Window Sampler that crops continuous streams into trainable units. We further design a Tri-modal Visual-Language Pretraining stage that aligns pose dynamics with textual semantics before joint optimization. To support comprehensive evaluation, we conduct experiments on two real-world streaming datasets, BOBSL and How2Sign, together with two newly constructed synthetic streaming benchmarks, Streaming-Phoenix-2014T and Streaming-CSL-Daily. The results demonstrate that StreamSLST consistently outperforms a matched cascaded segmentation-then-translation pipeline. Interestingly, our analysis reveals that joint optimization learns wider, translation-effective temporal support that simple expansion or rescaling of the annotated spans fails to reproduce. Our work establishes the first robust benchmark for continuous sign language understanding and paves the way for accessible real-world communication interfaces. The source code and data are available at this https URL.

Title: SemaNorm: Stain Normalization via Semantic and Morphology Alignment for Robust Cross-Hospital Computational Pathology

  • Authors: Subhankar Chattoraj, Sawon Pratiher, Arijit Ghosh, Prashant Khare, Writankar Kundu, Karan Vishwakarma, Preha Handa, Kirti Rashmi, Hubert Konik
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00056
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00056
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00056]] SemaNorm: Stain Normalization via Semantic and Morphology Alignment for Robust Cross-Hospital Computational Pathology(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00056)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Stain variability remains a major source of domain shift in computational pathology, yet existing normalization methods largely optimize color consistency without explicitly preserving morphology or diagnostic semantics. We propose SemaNorm, a self-supervised stain canonicalization stain normalizer to recover clean patches from strongly perturbed inputs, avoiding paired transfer, adversarial instability, and identity collapse. It further combines a frozen pathology foundation-model consistency loss with a differentiable nuclei-structure loss to preserve semantic and cellular organization. SemaNorm is evaluated across Camelyon17-WILDS and NCT-CRC-HE-100K using cross-hospital tumor classification, cell-graph morphology metrics, and semantic stability measured with an independent held-out foundation encoder. SemaNorm improves out-of-distribution accuracy by 5.9% over the strongest normalization baseline and by 13.5% over no normalization, while achieving the best morphology preservation. Independent foundation-model and cell-graph analyses further confirm improved semantic and structural stability. These results establish morphology- and semantics-aware optimization as a principled alternative to color-only stain normalization.

Title: CausalPrune: Counterfactual Causal Token Pruning for Efficient Large Vision-Language Models

  • Authors: Pengcheng Zheng, Chaoning Zhang, Ya Wen, Jiaxin Huang, Jiaquan Zhang, Caiyan Qin, Kuien Liu, Sheng Zheng, Pietro Liò, Yang Yang
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00058
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00058
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00058]] CausalPrune: Counterfactual Causal Token Pruning for Efficient Large Vision-Language Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00058)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) have achieved remarkable progress in multimodal understanding, but their practical deployment is still limited by the substantial computation introduced by excessive visual tokens. Visual token pruning offers an effective solution by removing redundant visual tokens during inference. However, existing methods typically estimate token importance from correlational statistics, which cannot distinguish tokens that causally determine the model output from those spuriously associated with it. To address this issue, we propose \textbf{CausalPrune}, a counterfactual causal token pruning framework for efficient LVLMs. We formulate LVLM inference as a Structural Causal Model and introduce the Causal Token Importance Score (CTIS), which estimates the Average Treatment Effect of intervening on each visual token. To make causal estimation practical, we derive an efficient first-order approximation of CTIS and incorporate backdoor adjustment to mitigate latent cross-modal confounding. Within this causal formulation, layer-wise causal mediation decomposes token importance into direct and indirect effects through intermediate hidden states, enabling progressive pruning across transformer layers in a principled manner. Extensive experiments on various LVLM architectures, such as LLaVA-1.5 and Qwen2.5-VL, demonstrate that CausalPrune maintains comparable or even superior multimodal performance while achieving substantial reductions in FLOPs and prefill latency, consistently outperforming existing pruning methods.

Title: Not All Minorities Are Equal: Fairness Outcomes in Private Image Generation

  • Authors: Cohen Archbold, Usman Hassan, Sen-ching Samson Cheung
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.CY
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00059
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00059
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00059]] Not All Minorities Are Equal: Fairness Outcomes in Private Image Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00059)
  • Keywords: privacy, protect, robust, fair, generative
  • Abstract: Differential privacy (DP) provides formal guarantees for protecting individuals in artificial intelligence systems, but its consequences for fairness in generative models remain poorly understood. We investigate how privacy-preserving training affects the representation of demographic groups and visual attributes in generative models of human faces. Across two facial image datasets and multiple privacy levels, we find that stronger privacy can disproportionately degrade the quality and utility of some underrepresented groups, suggesting that privacy can amplify existing disparities. However, representation alone does not explain these effects: several well-represented attributes also experience substantial degradation, while some underrepresented attributes remain comparatively robust. These findings show that the impact of privacy-preserving learning cannot be explained solely by data representation and may depend on the characteristics and interactions of the information being learned. Our results highlight the need to consider both privacy and fairness when evaluating generative models.

Title: DDEL: Closed-Loop Mutual Enhancement of Dataset Distillation and Ensemble Learning

  • Authors: Weiqi Zhang, Yongquan Yang
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00061
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00061
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00061]] DDEL: Closed-Loop Mutual Enhancement of Dataset Distillation and Ensemble Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00061)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Dataset distillation (DD) reduces a training set to a small set of examples, while ensemble learning (EL) combines predictions from several models. When applied in sequence, the distilled set remains fixed as the ensemble learns. We introduce DDEL, a framework that feeds ensemble behavior back into distillation. Each round trains the ensemble, measures loss and prediction disagreement on the source data, and uses these signals to guide the next data update. We implement this loop with distribution matching and a homogeneous three-member ensemble. Preliminary experiments cover skin lesion classification (ISIC 2019), treatment-response prediction (I-SPY1), and binary two-year prognosis classification (NSCLC-Radiomics). With the same images-per-class budget as the open-loop comparison, DDEL reaches balanced accuracy 0.3471 on ISIC and mean ROC-AUC 0.5120 and 0.5597 on I-SPY1 and NSCLC. The I-SPY1 difference is small, and the ISIC trajectory shows that more rounds do not consistently improve performance. These results offer initial evidence for studying DD-EL feedback, with its computational cost and robustness still requiring controlled evaluation.

Title: CarDiff: Anatomically Aware Dental Caries Synthesis using Diffusion

  • Authors: Sanyam Jain, Bruna Neves de Freitas, Alexandros Iosifidis, Ruben Pauwels

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00062

  • Keywords: diffusion, segmentation
  • Abstract: Synthetic medical image generation can help address limited annotated data in dental imaging, particularly for under-represented caries classes. In this work, we present CarDiff, a semi-supervised framework for dental caries synthesis in panoramic radiographs. The method combines paired mask-image learning with self-supervised generation from augmented masks, while incorporating anatomy-aware conditioning between lesion regions and surrounding structures. Semantic consistency is enforced through segmentation-guided supervision and adversarial anatomical alignment. Experiments on the MLUA-DC1000 dataset show that CarDiff achieves improved synthesis quality compared with existing semantic synthesis baselines, obtaining the best Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) (90.76) and Learned Perceptual Image Patch Similarity (LPIPS) (0.48) scores among evaluated methods. These results suggest that anatomically consistent diffusion synthesis may be useful for dental radiograph augmentation under limited supervision. The code is available at this https URL.

Title: OpenMidnight: Closing the Performance Gap Between Open and Proprietary Pathology Foundation Models

Kaplan, Chao Feng, Anish Dulal, Leema Krishna Murali, Rohitkumar Datchanamourty, Nevasini Sasikumar, Debojyoti Das, Ahmed Hassayoune, Rhizo Nymph, Anas Zafar, Connor Lane, Benjamin Warner, Hassan Muhammad, Tanishq Mathew Abraham, Paul S. We present OpenMidnight 1.1, an open-source replication and refinement of Midnight trained on approximately 12,000 publicly available whole-slide images from The Cancer Genome Atlas. Title: BreastXAgent: A lightweight agentic explainability framework for breast density classification

  • Authors: Muhammad Turab, Raja Vavekanand, Knooz Fatima
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00065
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00065

  • Keywords: explainability
  • Abstract: Deep learning models for breast density classification typically return only a label and confidence score. This paper presents BreastXAgent, a lightweight agentic framework that combines a pretrained classifier, Grad-CAM visual explanation, LangGraph-based routing, LLM report generation, and rule-based guardrails to produce controlled, faithful, non-diagnostic reports. On 160 mammography images, uncontrolled LLM reporting produced 75% Grad-CAM overclaims and 62.5% clinical overclaims. Strict prompting and LangGraph routing improved safety but did not eliminate violations. The full BreastXAgent achieved 100% safety pass rate, zero overclaims, and highest utility score. Results demonstrate that agentic guardrails enable safe, structured explanation generation without modifying the underlying classifier.

Title: Comparing Monocular and Multi-View 3D Pedestrian Reconstruction Strategies for Urban Intersection Sensing

  • Authors: Kauan Mariani, Matheus Carvalho, Joel Perca, João Rulff, Jorge Poco
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00066
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00066
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00066]] Comparing Monocular and Multi-View 3D Pedestrian Reconstruction Strategies for Urban Intersection Sensing(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00066)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Understanding how pedestrians move and interact at urban intersections is central to traffic-safety assessment, mobility planning, and smart-city sensing. Such analyses rely on physically grounded descriptions of motion: 3D body pose, metric trajectories, and pose-derived descriptors such as stature and walking speed. Recovering these from fixed roadside cameras is difficult: viewpoints are oblique and long-range, occlusions are frequent, and neither ground-truth 3D pose nor cross-camera identity labels are typically available. We compare two strategies for deriving pedestrian motion descriptors from a multi-camera intersection dataset: a monocular learned pipeline that combines text-prompted segmentation, tracking, and learned 3D body estimation, and a calibrated multi-view geometric pipeline that combines 2D pose estimation, tracking, cross-camera association, and triangulation. Because 3D ground truth is unavailable, we evaluate both with reference-free criteria relevant to intersection analysis: pedestrian coverage, anthropometric plausibility, walking-speed distributions, computational cost, and failure modes. The two strategies exhibit complementary trade-offs. The learned pipeline is simpler to deploy and covers more pedestrians, but suffers from scale ambiguity and systematic height compression; the geometric pipeline yields metric reconstructions more efficiently, but requires calibration, discards pedestrians seen by only one camera, and can produce severe outliers in dense crowds. We distill these findings into practical guidance for selecting a reconstruction strategy and configuring camera infrastructure according to the target analysis task.

Title: SAMP: Self-Adaptive Margin Prioritization For Robust Diffusion Watermarking

  • Authors: Yuhao Xue, Jiuan Zhou, Yu Cheng, Zhaoxia Yin
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00069
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00069
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00069]] SAMP: Self-Adaptive Margin Prioritization For Robust Diffusion Watermarking(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00069)
  • Keywords: attack, robust, watermark, diffusion
  • Abstract: Diffusion models have made high-fidelity visual generation widely accessible, while also lowering the barrier to producing convincing visual forgeries and complicating content provenance. Existing watermarking methods typically use a fixed embedding strength throughout denoising or treat all watermark dimensions equally. As a result, they may introduce unnecessary distortion when some watermark dimensions are already reliable, while failing to sufficiently reinforce those that are more vulnerable to subsequent perturbations. Therefore, robust watermarking requires adapting both the embedding strength during denoising and the optimization priority across watermark dimensions. To address this problem, we present SAMP, a self-adaptive framework consisting of global margin regulation and vulnerability-sensitive allocation. Global margin regulation adjusts the embedding strength according to the separation between the target codeword and competing codewords, together with the estimated effects of common attacks. Vulnerability-sensitive allocation identifies watermark dimensions that are more easily weakened by local perturbations and gives them higher optimization priority. Experimental results demonstrate that SAMP achieves an average information recovery accuracy of 0.993. Compared with state-of-the-art methods, SAMP improves PSNR by 16.8% and reduces LPIPS by 47.1%. These results validate the effectiveness of SAMP for reliable and high-quality diffusion watermarking.

  • Abstract: Drone-based person detection is being adopted by emergency-management agencies to find missing people faster than ground teams can. Title: Demystifying Prompt Stealing Attacks against Synthetic Video

  • Authors: Junyan Huang, Sanskar Agmain, Chen Gong, Yan Pang, FNU Suya
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00102
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00102
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00102]] Demystifying Prompt Stealing Attacks against Synthetic Video(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00102)
  • Keywords: attack, steal, generative
  • Abstract: Creators publicly showcase generated content to advertise and sell their work, but these examples may also enable unauthorized reproduction and customization. We study whether an adversary can derive reusable inputs from a single public video for faithful reconstruction and controlled editing, without access to the original prompt or generator. We adapt captioning and prompt optimization approaches to video prompt stealing attacks (PSAs) and find that prompt recovery alone provides limited reconstruction fidelity. Even the original prompt produces different spatial and temporal realizations when the initial noise changes. Using an independent generator enlarges the reconstruction gap, as reusing the original prompt and seed can still produce substantially different videos. We propose V-PSA, which uses a recovered caption and DDIM inversion to construct a reference trajectory under an independent generator. Null-Text Inversion then optimizes per-step unconditional embeddings to reduce deviations from this trajectory during reconstruction. The resulting inputs also guide generation when the caption is modified, supporting edits that change selected content while preserving the target's layout and motion. We construct a benchmark containing 12,000 video-prompt pairs from three victim generators and four prompt sources. V-PSA achieves video-to-video similarity around 0.9 between target and reconstructed videos, substantially improving over prompt-only baselines with similarity score of 0.6, and supports multiple controlled variations that preserve the target's structure. Additional experiments show that augmenting prior image PSAs with inversion-derived inputs also improves reconstruction. Our results show that evaluations restricted to prompt recovery can underestimate an attacker's ability to reconstruct public generative outputs.

  • Abstract: Existing large language model (LLM) backdoor attacks operate primarily in chat settings, where the risk is bounded by free-form text outputs. Overall, we demonstrate that LLM backdoors can have critical security impacts in the context of modern coding agents, and we characterize the poisoning methodology required to insert backdoors that are both effective and stealthy in this setting.

  • Abstract: Graph-aware calibrators make graph neural networks (GNNs) more trustworthy by predicting a temperature for each node, usually with auxiliary networks whose decisions are hard to inspect. We propose Interpretable Structural Temperature Scaling (ISTS), a twelve-parameter linear model that sets each node's temperature from named descriptors of its confidence, neighbourhood and closeness to labelled nodes, so every temperature traces back to descriptor contributions. ISTS preserves predictions, and cross-validated regularisation lets the calibration data decide how far the model departs from temperature scaling (TS). Its explanations are faithful at the level of node groups: on test nodes, ISTS softens groups that a global temperature leaves over-confident and sharpens under-confident ones (Spearman correlation 0.67 over 246 groups). On seven graphs with two backbones and eleven baselines, ISTS has the best average rank in negative log-likelihood (NLL) and Brier score and lowers NLL relative to TS by 1.8%, more than twice the gain of an identically fitted entropy-based temperature. It ranked first again on four held-out graphs under a protocol fixed in advance. From about 100 calibration nodes on, ISTS is a cheap and inspectable choice, and on point-cloud segmentation it significantly improves NLL, Brier score and the ranking of points for rejection.

Title: KINDER: Kernel-based Independence for Fair Representation Learning via Prototype-space Erasure

  • Authors: Abtin Mogharabin, Alp Toykan Kaplan, Jiaee Cheong, Sinan Kalkan
  • Subjects: cs.LG, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00264
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00264
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00264]] KINDER: Kernel-based Independence for Fair Representation Learning via Prototype-space Erasure(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00264)
  • Keywords: protect, fair, segmentation
  • Abstract: Concept erasure is a prominent approach to achieving fairness in machine learning through the removal of sensitive attributes from learned representations. Prior concept erasure methods typically define debiasing indirectly through the failure of a chosen adversary, probe, or fairness penalty, which makes the target of erasure dependent on a particular decoder family or optimization setup. We introduce KINDER, which directly removes sensitive-attribute information from intermediate representations with respect to a chosen family of prototype functionals, and is compatible with any deep model containing an intermediate feature space, independent of the architecture, task type, or training paradigm. KINDER operates in a random Fourier feature space, where it estimates a sensitive subspace from protected group prototypes and projects representations onto its orthogonal complement. Thus, it forms an explicit representation cleaning mechanism distinct from prior fairness control studies. We conduct extensive experiments across diverse settings and show the effectiveness of KINDER on (i) supervised and self-supervised settings, (ii) classification, regression, and image segmentation tasks, and (iii) diverse data modalities, including visual, textual, and tabular data. Code and trained models are available at: this https URL

  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Post-training quantization (PTQ) reduces the memory burden of large model deployment by casting the high precision trained weights to fewer bits. While this promotes memory-efficient inference, it also incurs quantization error that impairs model performance. To compensate for quantization error, the present contribution decomposes the original trained weights into a quantized matrix, a low-rank component, and a sparse matrix that accounts for outlier rows in the weights. Due to analytical intractability and the discrete structure of the decomposition, a simulated annealing search is employed to explore candidate decompositions while avoiding poor local minima. The novel method can markedly outperform direct quantization while incurring minimal memory overhead.

Title: Seeing the Unseen: Unified Visible--Invisible Motion for Physically Consistent Video Generation

  • Authors: Mingyang Bao, Fangda Ye, Xihao Chen, Bobo Li, Jason Li, Shengqiong Wu
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00293
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00293
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00293]] Seeing the Unseen: Unified Visible--Invisible Motion for Physically Consistent Video Generation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00293)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Recent advances in video generation have achieved impressive visual quality, yet they often fail to produce physically consistent dynamics due to the lack of explicit modeling of underlying physical processes. In this work, we propose Seeing the Unseen, a unified framework for physics-grounded video generation. Our approach decomposes the problem into two stages: learning physical dynamics and injecting them into video synthesis. By integrating these trajectories into a diffusion-based generator, our method produces videos that are both visually coherent and physically consistent. Extensive experiments demonstrate improved motion accuracy, interaction consistency, and reduced physical artifacts compared to strong baselines.

We evaluate adversarial hull patches and find they suppress detection in up to 29% of attempts but never cause misclassification as benign. Title: Unlearning by Extrapolating a Shrink-and-Heal Lookahead

  • Authors: Hoang-Chau Luong, Lingwei Chen
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00374
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00374
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00374]] Unlearning by Extrapolating a Shrink-and-Heal Lookahead(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00374)
  • Keywords: attack, large language model
  • Abstract: Unlearning aims to make a large language model behave like one retrained without its forget set. Prevailing likelihood-suppression objectives, however, lack an explicit forgetting target: they continually reward lower forget-set likelihood, making forgetting sensitive to the optimization budget. We address this limitation by estimating the direction toward the oracle without training it. Our key idea is to shrink the fine-tuned model toward its pre-fine-tuning weights and briefly re-learn the retain set, thereby removing the forget-specific contribution; in the linearized regime, this shrink-and-heal lookahead moves the model a fixed fraction of the way to the retrained model, its unique fixed point. Our method, SHIFT, extrapolates the lookahead's per-token changes into distillation targets and iteratively refines them. Evaluated against eleven baselines under a unified OpenUnlearning protocol, SHIFT achieves the highest aggregate score across all TOFU settings and the closest oracle alignment, 3.7x closer on average than the next-best method; on MUSE, it preserves the most retain knowledge. Under larger optimization budgets, baselines overshoot the oracle, while SHIFT remains near it, even under relearning attacks.

Title: Spoofing the Latent State: Imperceptible Goal-Image Attacks Derail Planning in LeWorldModel

  • Authors: Kunal Bhosikar, Nicole Meng, Charu Sharma, Yingjie Lao
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.CR, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00427
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00427
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00427]] Spoofing the Latent State: Imperceptible Goal-Image Attacks Derail Planning in LeWorldModel(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00427)
  • Keywords: attack, robust
  • Abstract: Joint-embedding predictive architectures (JEPAs) have recently emerged as general-purpose visual world models for control. In this work, we investigate the test-time robustness of this latent planning interface. Analyzing the learned metric on Push-T, we first find that task-relevant object state heavily dominates latent distance: shifting the block by a single pixel displaces the embedding $3\times$ further than an equivalent agent shift, and swapping the block pose alone accounts for $99\%$ of the distance between scene pairs. However, because this pose information is distributed across a curved, high-dimensional manifold rather than a fixed linear subspace, the encoder cannot be trivially edited post-hoc to isolate or redirect it. We next demonstrate that this geometry is highly vulnerable at test time. A white-box $\ell_\infty$ perturbation of just $\epsilon{=}4/255$ applied to a single input frame causes the encoder to register the block at an arbitrary, attacker-chosen pose in $73$--$95\%$ of scenes without disturbing the encoded agent state. Perturbing either the goal image or the live camera observation drops Push-T planning success from $87\%$ to $32\%$, whereas uniform random noise at twice the magnitude has negligible impact. This vulnerability persists across all four standard LeWM benchmarks (Push-T, Two-Room, Reacher, and OGBench-Cube). At $\epsilon{=}8/255$, the attack bridges $82$--$97\%$ of the squared latent distance to the target embedding in every environment, inducing planning degradation proportional to each task's reliance on the spoofed entity.

To address this, we present Real Video Amodal Segmentation (Real-VAS), a new large-scale, zero-shot evaluation dataset built from composited real-image video. We introduce a novel data generation pipeline that enables the creation of programmatically consistent amodal ground truth for real-world footage, in which the object mask, occlusion boundary, and composited image are all derived from a single unoccluded source, without relying on human estimation or expensive 3D reconstruction. Our dataset is structured into two challenging scenarios: dynamic Occlusion, created by compositing two clips of moving objects, and a unique Container category featuring complex, physically constrained interactions. These container scenarios--On Surface Containment, Articulated Containment, and Mobile Containment--allow us to generate precise ground truth by simulating an object's motion based on its container's tracked transformation. As a result, Real-VAS provides a diverse and challenging benchmark for evaluating amodal segmentation models on realistic video with the precision of synthetic data. The dataset and our generation code will be made publicly available.

Title: Statistical Inference under Performativity: From Policy Feedback to Self-Evolving Systems

  • Authors: Tianwei Gao, Garrett G. Wen, Huiying Zhong, Lihua Lei, Weijie Su, Zhun Deng
  • Subjects: cs.LG, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00438
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00438
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00438]] Statistical Inference under Performativity: From Policy Feedback to Self-Evolving Systems(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00438)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Performativity arises when prediction-informed decisions change the data-generating process, creating feedback between deployment and learning. This phenomenon occurs in policy-making and in self-evolving AI systems that shape their own future training data. Statistical inference is challenging because estimation errors in earlier predictors affect subsequent data distributions and propagate into later updates. We develop an end-to-end framework for statistical inference under performativity. For repeated risk minimization and repeated gradient descent, we establish central limit theorems at fixed deployment rounds and derive covariance recursions that capture both current-round sampling variation and uncertainty inherited from earlier deployments. We develop consistent covariance estimators using policy perturbations and score matching. These results can yield asymptotically valid confidence regions for population iterates and the performative stable point. We further extend prediction-powered inference to this dynamic setting, combining labeled observations with predictions on additional unlabeled data to improve statistical efficiency. We evaluate our framework in synthetic, semi-synthetic educational, and self-evolving large language model routing settings. Our experiments demonstrate that ignoring propagated uncertainty can lead to substantial undercoverage even after repeated retraining. Together, these results provide statistical tools for learning systems that help determine the distributions from which they learn. Our work establish a complete statistical inference under performativity, introducing new challenges and inference settings that we believe will provide substantial value to policy-making, statistics, and machine learning.

  • Abstract: High-resolution video diffusion relies on attention over long token sequences, making attention efficiency critical to generation latency.   • Authors: Changjiang Han, Bowei He, Ye Yuan, Weixu Zhang, Haolun Wu, Xue Liu, Jikun Kang

These findings validate our motivation of treating retrieval as a learning resource, with parametric memory shaped by how retrieved knowledge is used to inform the model's future predictions. Code and model weights are available at this https URL and this https URL.

Title: Beyond Blackwell: Constrained Data Processing Inequalities

  • Authors: Brendan van Rooyen
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00510
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00510
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00510]] Beyond Blackwell: Constrained Data Processing Inequalities(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00510)
  • Keywords: fair
  • Abstract: We study the comparison of statistical experiments from a decision-theoretic standpoint, working with Markov transitions as basis-free linear maps on dual spaces. Our starting observation is that the data-processing inequality, that adding noise to an experiment cannot lower its Bayes risk, is the functoriality of fibrewise minimisation over a presheaf of decision rules: the rules available after an experiment form a presheaf, garbling acts on it by precomposition, and a risk functional is a natural transformation from it to the ordered reals. The argument refers to neither probabilities nor expected values, and it survives replacing the decision presheaf by a sub-presheaf of admissible rules. Any constraint imposed on the strategy that a rule induces fairness, unbiasedness, is automatically of this form, and so yields a data-processing inequality with no counterpart in the unconstrained Blackwell theory. Our main result is their quantitative companion. For constraints closed under post-processing we define a constrained deficiency and prove a constrained randomization theorem: one experiment dominates another on every admissible decision problem exactly when the constrained deficiency is small. The classical theorem is the case of no constraint, where the identity is itself an admissible rule; in general it is not, and the constrained deficiency is an optimisation over the coarsenings of an experiment that render it compliant. Specialising to equalized odds, we exhibit a pair of experiments that unconstrained Bayes risk identifies at a given loss and prior but that the constrained risk separates, giving a one-line certificate of Blackwell-incomparability. We work throughout with finite sets.

Title: Efficient and Scalable Multi-View Masked Autoencoder for Heterogeneous Graph Self-Supervised Learning

  • Authors: Hao Geng, Deqing Wang, Lihao Ge, Chenguang Du, Fuzhen Zhuang, Bowen Yang, Yanlin Hu, Songtao Wang
  • Subjects: cs.LG

  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00511

  • Keywords: robust, transformer
  • Abstract: Recent years have witnessed the flourishing of self-supervised learning on heterogeneous graphs, e.g., masked autoencoder-based methods. However, existing methods suffer from three issues, including the prohibitive computational cost of constructing meta-path-induced subgraphs, the limitations of uniform random masking, and the suboptimal objective of reconstructing potentially noisy or uninformative original node features. To address these issues, we propose the Multi-View Masked Auto Encoder (MVMAE), a novel, efficient, and scalable framework for heterogeneous graph self-supervised learning. First, MVMAE introduces a novel multi-view mask-then-reconstruct strategy. This approach shifts the learning objective to a high-level and semantic task, providing a more robust and informative supervisory signal that is effective even when initial node features are absent or flawed. Then, to handle the computational demands, MVMAE employs an efficient offline aggregation mechanism that pre-computes and reuses representations for each meta-path view, completely avoiding the costly construction of meta-path-induced subgraphs. Next, to fuse the representations of each view, MVMAE uses a Transformer-based multi-view fusion encoder to capture the complex correlations between views, and a deliberately simple MLP decoder to compel the encoder to learn highly expressive and robust node representations. Extensive experiments on six real-world datasets, including three large-scale graphs, demonstrate that MVMAE consistently outperforms state-of-the-art self-supervised and even semi-supervised baselines, confirming its superior effectiveness and scalability. Our source code is released at this https URL.

Title: Practical Preimages for 19- and 20-Step SHA-256 with Standard Padding

  • Abstract: We present practical preimage attacks on 19- and 20-step SHA-256 that respect the standard IV, Davies-Meyer feed-forward, and full FIPS 180-4 padding, for arbitrary prescribed 256-bit targets. A companion Zero-$a_3$ bridge inverts the unpadded 20-step compression function in $2^{38.1}$ evaluations. Title: Conic Certification of Transformer Attention

  • Authors: Masoud Ataei
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL, math.OC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00585
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00585
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00585]] Conic Certification of Transformer Attention(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00585)
  • Keywords: transformer, large language model
  • Abstract: Attention is the computational core of large language models, yet the verifiers that bound how far an attention head's output can move under input perturbations give no guarantee on how loose their bounds are. A weak certificate therefore cannot be told apart from a fragile model. We develop a conic programming theory of attention certification that supplies this guarantee. We prove that the difficulty lies not in the softmax but in the bilinear interaction of a varying query with the keys. This interaction makes certification NP-hard over boxes and inapproximable once the attention logits can move by a constant amount. A fixed query or listed token substitutions, by contrast, make the exact worst case efficiently computable. An exponential cone moment relaxation then certifies a head with an error bounded quadratically in the perturbation radius by computable quantities, an order that cannot be improved. We also show that relaxations that treat a network one operation at a time, linear bound propagation among them, pay on second-order terms a sum of per-operation maxima instead of one coupled maximum. Computational results on GPT-2 indicate that the theory carries over to a trained model. Against exact worst cases with the query fixed, the certificate attains the predicted second-order accuracy. It is tighter than linear bound propagation on every head, and the looseness of bound propagation tracks the predicted per-operation excess.

Title: Dataset Mismatch Matters in Group Relative Policy Optimization for Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards

  • Authors: Zhen-Yu Zhang, Jiandong Zhang, Huaxiu Yao, Gang Niu, Masashi Sugiyama
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00586
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00586
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00586]] Dataset Mismatch Matters in Group Relative Policy Optimization for Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00586)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: Recently, reinforcement learning from verifiable rewards (RLVR) is practically very important, where the leading approach is group relative policy optimization (GRPO) that typically faces a trade-off between rollout efficiency and downstream performance. To improve efficiency while preserving performance, existing selective rollout methods mainly exploit only source-side training dynamics to optimize the discrete distribution over source prompts. However, in practice, the source training dataset is rarely identically distributed to the target test dataset. When such a mismatch exists, due to legal or privacy constraints, it is usually not acceptable to share target prompts themselves with the RLVR trainer. Nevertheless, it is still possible for the target data owner to provide limited aggregated feedback from a small held-out target set without revealing target prompts or rollout traces. In this paper, we propose learning-to-reweight rollout (L2RR) that explicitly incorporates target-side performance feedback into GRPO. Specifically, L2RR learns a discrete sampling distribution over source prompts by optimizing a bi-level objective that maximizes accuracy on a held-out target dataset, while simultaneously penalizing rollouts that provide little incremental learning information. Empirical studies on six math reasoning benchmarks and three model scales show that L2RR improves target-domain performance and increases rollout efficiency.

Title: Do Orthogonality Constraints Help Language-Model Training Beyond Sphere Optimization?

  • Authors: Kai Hu, Li Zhang, Weichen Yu, Matt Fredrikson, Haoqi Hu
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00593
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00593
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00593]] Do Orthogonality Constraints Help Language-Model Training Beyond Sphere Optimization?(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00593)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer, large language model
  • Abstract: A growing body of work reports benefits from orthogonality constraints in fine-tuning large language models (LLMs). These results raise an open question: do orthogonality constraints also help LLM pre-training? We conduct a systematic empirical study of decoder-only LLM pre-training with models ranging from 360M to 3.1B parameters, trained on nearly 200B tokens. We examine fourteen variants of orthogonality training: some constrain the weight matrix itself, while others use orthogonal factors to reparameterize it. Because a strictly orthogonal square matrix loses half of its degrees of freedom, we also study block-wise orthogonality, where every $d$ rows form a row-orthonormal (Stiefel) matrix and the block size $d$ is a hyperparameter. Among block sizes ranging from 1 to the shorter side of the matrix, no $d>1$ used throughout the network reliably outperforms $d=1$, and large blocks are clearly worse; the one placement that helps, symmetric positive-definite factors on the query and key projections, loses its small gain as models grow. At $d=1$, the constraint reduces to sphere optimization, with each row direction on its own unit sphere, which by itself already improves on tuned Muon. The same two findings hold for a diffusion transformer trained on ImageNet, and after fine-tuning, downstream accuracy does not separate the parameterizations. Our conclusion is that, across the realizations we tried, orthogonality between rows does not help language-model pre-training beyond sphere optimization. Since this appears to conflict with several prior results, we re-examine five prior methods in pre-training and fine-tuning that report orthogonality improvements. In each case the reported gain is attributable to numerical precision, training horizon, optimizer realization, or per-row norm control rather than to the orthogonality the methods impose.

  • Abstract: Existing studies of vulnerable pre-installed Android apps identify dangerous behavior by checking whether an app invokes an API from a predefined set of security-sensitive APIs. Title: Enhancing Language Model Reasoning with Structured Multi-Level Modeling

  • Authors: Siheng Xiong, Ali Payani, Faramarz Fekri
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00616
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00616

  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Inference-time scaling enhances a model's reasoning by extending its chain-of-thought (CoT). To address this, we propose Multi-Level Reasoning (MLR), which reformulates long-CoT generation as a two-level stochastic process. A high-level planner generates structured step descriptors specifying both the reasoning mode and the semantic subgoal. We therefore introduce iterative Step-DPO, a process-level preference optimization scheme that leverages Twisted Sequential Monte Carlo (TSMC) to provide scalable stepwise supervision. This yields more effective training, improved stability, and higher accuracy. Extensive experiments on challenging math, science, and logical reasoning benchmarks show that, under the same reduced data budget (10% SFT and 5% preference relative to the DeepSeek-R1 distillation setup), MLR outperforms both SFT-based distillation and strong RL/preference-optimization baselines across multiple base models and tasks. Moreover, MLR exhibits slower performance degradation on long-horizon reasoning, demonstrating stronger robustness under extended CoT generation.

Title: Bridging Singular Learning Theory and Mechanistic Interpretability at the Billion-Parameter Scale

  • Authors: Ethan Ji, Wei Wang, Yizhou Sun
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00640
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00640
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00640]] Bridging Singular Learning Theory and Mechanistic Interpretability at the Billion-Parameter Scale(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00640)
  • Keywords: interpretability
  • Abstract: Mechanistic interpretability (MI) explains a computation in a trained network by identifying the components and variables that implement it and testing them with interventions. Singular learning theory (SLT) describes the same network through the geometry of the loss around its parameters, summarized by the local learning coefficient. In pretrained language models the two have not been compared: SLT's estimates at this scale concern whole models or components whose roles were not independently established, and the estimator, which averages the loss over parameter settings drawn near the trained ones, has had no reference for how far those draws may move the parameters. We show that both are functions of one object, the map from a component's parameters to the model's predictions under inputs and interventions, whose first derivatives determine the Fisher information, the learning coefficient, and intervention effects, and whose higher derivatives add a quartic correction and pairwise terms. We test this correspondence in Pythia-1B on Dyck bracket matching at all 154 training checkpoints, against a five-stage circuit identified by interventions. We measure the local geometry of all 144 components inside the region where predictions are preserved, with the Fisher spectrum at every checkpoint, which gives the quadratic part of the learning-coefficient estimate at every temperature. Changes of the Fisher trace track changes of causal effect (median partial rank correlation 0.51), largely as a trend shared across the model, whereas the gain Fisher of a component's output and the large-temperature count are specific to the component. The count follows causal change across the population but not the growth of a formed circuit component. The quartic part is what a sampler that leaves the region registers beyond curvature. Pairwise interaction curvature tracks joint-ablation interactions.

Title: Inference First, Training Underneath: Layer-Granular LoRA Co-Serving with LayerLoom

  • Authors: Zongpu Zhang, Yishu Yin, Xuehai Qian
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00652
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00652
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00652]] Inference First, Training Underneath: Layer-Granular LoRA Co-Serving with LayerLoom(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00652)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Serving and fine-tuning a large language model on the same GPUs can turn the idle capacity between inference bursts into training progress, and low-rank adaptation (LoRA) makes this co-location cheap because both workloads read a single frozen copy of the base weights. The difficulty lies in the fine-tuning backward pass, which has no counterpart in inference. Current co-serving systems schedule backward work per serving iteration: each iteration carries a fixed backward budget, and requests that arrive in the meantime wait for it to finish, so fine-tuning dictates inference latency whenever load spikes. We observe that the backward pass of a LoRA microbatch decomposes into per-layer tasks over the same weights that the inference forward pass visits, which allows backward work to be scheduled at layer rather than iteration granularity. Building on this observation, LayerLoom attaches same-layer backward tasks to the inference forward pass and, at each layer, runs only as many of them as the latency slack of in-flight requests permits. To keep a ready task at every layer, LayerLoom maintains a wavefront of fine-tuning microbatches staggered across layers, and it bounds the wavefront's activation memory by offloading microbatch state to host memory and prefetching it ahead of the layer where the scheduler plans to use it. A fused kernel serves the paired forward and backward GEMMs from a single read of each base weight. Because all backward work of an iteration finishes within its forward pass, new requests join the next iteration as in continuous batching. On an RTX 4090 GPU running Llama-3.1-8B under a bursty production trace, LayerLoom raises joint SLO attainment by 13.5-21.8 points over prior works with fine-tuning throughput that is comparable or up to 12% higher, and it reaches the same training losses.

Title: Regularized Wasserstein Distance for Discrete Generative Modeling and Clustering Analysis

  • Authors: Senmao Ye, Fei Liu, Dawei Zhang, Yuqian Zheng, Jun Zhang, Zhonglong zheng
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00653
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00653
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00653]] Regularized Wasserstein Distance for Discrete Generative Modeling and Clustering Analysis(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00653)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: In this paper, we formulate clustering analysis as discrete generative modeling. Specifically, when the generator is restricted to a finite candidate space, the generated samples naturally emerge as the most representative prototypes of the underlying data distribution. To model such a discrete candidate space, we employ a Discrete Distribution Network (DDN). However, DDN suffers from unstable training due to its hard nearest-neighbor selection. To stabilize training and encourage geometric consistency, we replace DDN's l2 loss between images with regularized Wasserstein distance between the empirical distributions of real and generated images. The resulting framework, termed Discrete Generative Clustering (DGC), offers four notable advantages: 1) the learned cluster centers are directly interpretable, as they are realistic synthetic samples; 2) the inference cost scales logarithmically with the number of clusters, i.e., O(log K), making the method scalable to extremely large K; 3) the regularized Wasserstein distance provides a smooth and well-behaved optimization objective that facilitates stable convergence, whereas DDN's l2 loss tends to converge to poor local optima; and 4) DGC replaces DDN's nearest-neighbor selection with random-neighbor selection, which avoids the density shift problem during multi-step forward. Extensive experiments on several benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness and desirable properties of DGC. The code is publicly available at GitHub.

Title: Generative Forecasting from Coarse-Grained Observations: When Does Cascading Help?

  • Authors: Alexandra Spitzer, Giovanni Abati, Lorenzo Baldassari, Till Muser, Cheng Shi, Ivan Dokmanić
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00686
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00686
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00686]] Generative Forecasting from Coarse-Grained Observations: When Does Cascading Help?(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00686)
  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: We study probabilistic forecasting of dynamical systems such as fluids and waves from coarse observations. Since the unseen fine scales influence the dynamics, the same coarse initial state can evolve into a whole distribution of futures. Generative forecasters may produce visually plausible samples, but it is hard to tell whether they sample the right conditional distribution, since trajectory data contain a single future for each initial state. We address this problem by generating reference data in a hierarchical way: for each coarse initial state, we generate many different fine-scale components and numerically evolve the full states. Using these conditional ensembles, we show that standard diffusion models and stochastic interpolants make substantial distributional errors. We then show that cascading shorter forecasts greatly reduces these errors for the turbulent flows and waves we study, but we also find and characterize cases in which it hurts. Since the coarse process is not Markov, cascading discards information about the unresolved fine scales even with exact transitions. To understand this, we propose a solvable analytic model which shows that the optimal cascade depth decreases as the coupling between the unresolved and observed scales gets stronger, and increases as the dependence of the forecast on the observation becomes more oscillatory. Consistent with our experiments, cascading is detrimental when the direct forecast is ``easy'' to learn, for example Gaussian as in the passive scalar case. We release our datasets as a first benchmark for probabilistic forecasting with ground-truth conditional ensembles.

Because this structure is a property of the landscape's shape rather than of any particular coordinates, it can be read off directly with topology and should persist across feature spaces. We build this landscape from frame-by-frame trajectories, extract its discrete-Morse graph, and keep two kinds of structure: paths, the corridors a trajectory follows between dense behavioral regions, and cycles, closed routes whose entry and exit take different ways. Clustering these structures yields transitions directly, with no upstream state partition. On a rat behavior dataset across three feature spaces, including the latent space of a neural-network controller that imitates the animal in a physics simulator, the approach recovers transitions at coverage comparable to state-space and topological-clustering baselines, while carrying substantially more information about the transition identity, forming clusters at least as tight as the baselines', and matching annotated transitions with less reliance on time-warping. More broadly, the results show that behavioral transitions are a topological feature of a representation: recoverable from the shape of its trajectories, and consistent across distinct feature spaces.

Robustness exceeds LOAO detection for every model (gaps of 24 to 72 percentage points, 23 to 66 when robustness is also averaged over families; Holm-adjusted p $\le$ 0.00096), and the two measures agree at most on a coarse ranking of the models. An exact mixed-integer evasion search confirms the tree ensembles' scores. Benign-only anomaly detectors and a simple multi-class open-set baseline do not close the gap. A temporal split on 5 seeds (3 of the 4 models) leaves the gap in place. On CSE-CIC-IDS2018 the gap recurs for every model (16 to 68 points). On these benchmarks and under this threat model, a robustness score should not be read as a zero-day score, and the two should be reported separately. Code and per-seed results are publicly available.

Title: Adversarial robustness does not entail human-aligned categorization

  • Authors: Itay Reznik, Raz Lapid, Moshe Sipper, Tal Golan
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG, q-bio.NC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00715
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00715
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00715]] Adversarial robustness does not entail human-aligned categorization(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00715)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Adversarial training can improve some forms of correspondence between artificial and biological vision, but whether robust classifiers acquire human-aligned classification behavior remains unclear. We generated 1,008 controversial stimuli by optimizing latent inputs to a pretrained GAN to elicit high-confidence disagreements between pairs of robust ImageNet classifiers. We compared four adversarially robust ImageNet classifiers - ResNet-101, ViT-B, ConvNeXt-L, and Swin-L - with two-alternative category judgments from 60 participants. We first evaluated each classifier on stimuli optimized to elicit disagreement with another model. Across classifiers, accuracy in predicting individual participants' single-trial category choices was near chance (48.6-51.3%), whereas majority judgments from other participants predicted responses with 85.0-86.8% accuracy. An analysis incorporating model prediction strength and participants' reported confidence found weak positive model-human correspondence for the two convolutional models. On stimuli optimized for disagreement between other model pairs, all four robust classifiers predicted human responses above chance but less accurately than predictions based on other participants' majority judgments; the same pattern held for 17 additional RobustBench models not used in stimulus generation. Across these 17 models, mean model-participant agreement was 67.3%, versus 81.3% mean participant-participant agreement on the same images. These results identify model-specific failures of human alignment and a model-human mismatch that persists across robust classifiers and substantially exceeds disagreement among human observers. Our findings show that adversarial robustness alone does not guarantee human-aligned categorization and suggest that robustness and human alignment should be evaluated as distinct properties of visual recognition systems.

Title: The Long Con on the Web: Earn-then-Exploit Fraud Attacks against Coding Agents

  • Authors: Xingling Xu, Zhengwei Jiang, Zheng Zhi, Fangming Dong, LingJia Guo, Wei Dong, Chunyan Ma, Jun Jiang
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00725
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00725
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00725]] The Long Con on the Web: Earn-then-Exploit Fraud Attacks against Coding Agents(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00725)
  • Keywords: defense, attack
  • Abstract: Coding agents increasingly operate with web access and high privileges, creating a new attack surface where web fraud can directly target autonomous agents. Existing studies mainly focus on malicious links, prompt injection, and interface deception, while content-level web fraud against agents and the amplification of attacks through agent trust remain underexplored. In this paper, we present EtE (Earn-then-Exploit), a web fraud attack framework against coding agents, in which a malicious website first provides multiple rounds of genuinely verifiable assistance to establish trust and then introduces task-related instructions that cross intended operational boundaries. To systematically evaluate its effectiveness, we construct 56 organizational environments (8 task contexts x 7 attack consequences) and evaluate three representative coding agents. EtE achieves an average scene-level attack success rate of 71.4%, including 85.7% on Codex-GPT-5.5, 67.9% on ZCode-GLM-5.3-Flash, and 60.7% on QwenCode-Qwen3.7-Plus, significantly outperforming all injection-based baselines. More importantly, our experiments reveal that coding agents exhibit trust-like behavioral reliance on previously helpful Web sources, and that such accumulated credibility significantly increases their susceptibility to subsequent malicious instructions. Based on this finding, we propose action-authorization separation (AAS), which prevents web content from directly authorizing privileged actions and reduces the attack success rate to 0% while preserving 100% task completion, outperforming all evaluated defense baselines.

Title: Constructing Budgeted KGW Evasion through Token-Event Evidence Replacement

  • Authors: Hongliang Liu

  • Abstract: KGW watermarks bias sampling to accumulate keyed detection evidence. We construct budgeted evasion through token-event evidence replacement using ordinary phrase rewrites. At the largest attainable cost within the budget, exact coverage search maximizes removal of original scored token pairs. It uses a known tokenizer without the key, detector feedback or queries to the watermarked model.
