Comparisons did not establish an advantage over simpler selection and ordinary constrained rewriting. The construction makes budgeted evidence replacement executable while exposing limits in signed reduction and recognition of sufficient editing.

Title: AgentCap: Capability-Based Runtime Authorization for Secure LLM Agent Execution

  • Authors: Aravinda Kashyap Kabbinamakki Suresh, Peng Gao
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00729
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00729
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00729]] AgentCap: Capability-Based Runtime Authorization for Secure LLM Agent Execution(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00729)
  • Keywords: secure, security, attack
  • Abstract: Language-model agents commonly collapse two decisions that security systems separate: selecting an action and determining whether that action is authorized. When a model exercises standing authority, indirect prompt injection can convert influence over its reasoning into unauthorized effects. We present AgentCap, a runtime authorization layer that externalizes this decision from the model and mediates every tool invocation through an MCP wrapper. Each request declares an intent, and AgentCap evaluates policy constraints over trust, workflow prerequisites, mutually exclusive authority, and the provenance of security-sensitive arguments. Authorized requests receive short-lived, budget-bounded capability tokens that are validated at the tool boundary. Delegation is restricted to session-bound parent-child chains that preserve action and resource while budget and lifetime can only be attenuated, preventing authority from being expanded or reused across unrelated agent sessions. A declarative policy language provides a common representation for runtime enforcement and predeployment analysis. Across AgentDojo and InjecAgent, AgentCap reduces attack success in every evaluated model-benchmark setting, including zero attack success with GPT-5.4 on both benchmarks. Controlled adversarial scenarios further show that AgentCap blocks every attempted unauthorized execution (ASR 0%) while preserving all legitimate operations exercised. These results demonstrate that agent security requires authorization of model-proposed effects at the tool boundary, independently of the model's reasoning.

Title: The Tangled Web They Weave: Exploring Neural Representations with Directed Simplicial Complexes

  • Authors: Michael Bleher
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00786
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00786
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00786]] The Tangled Web They Weave: Exploring Neural Representations with Directed Simplicial Complexes(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00786)
  • Keywords: interpretability
  • Abstract: Understanding how artificial neural networks (ANNs) represent and combine features internally is a central challenge in mechanistic interpretability. Existing neural circuit-discovery methods represent neuron interactions as pairwise edges in a graph. But distributed neural representations consist of large groups of neurons that are inherently linked by higher-order causal dependencies: suppressing a subgroup of neurons in concert may shift the activation of other neurons in the feature representation. We propose a method to construct filtered directed simplicial complexes (DSCs) that give a topological representation of such dependencies, built from the activation profiles of an ANN on a given set of inputs. Our approach treats ANNs as structural causal models and uses counterfactual ablations to test for causal links between groups of internal neurons within specific input contexts. As a proof of concept, we apply the approach to a pair of small multilayer perceptron (MLP) architectures in a synthetic two-dimensional quadrant classification task. In these examples, DSCs recover the designed feature pathways and coordinated class transitions, and the complexes of trained networks are higher-dimensional than those of their handcrafted references. As validation, we compare our method with graph-based circuit-discovery baselines and find that their 1-skeletons agree within the relations that a weight graph can represent. These results support DSCs as context-dependent descriptions of multi-neuron computation and may thus provide a novel, orthogonal framework to study the phenomenon of distributed feature representations and polysemanticity.

Title: Crop Mapping Evaluation under Sampling Bias

  • Authors: Akram Zaytar, Kemunto Ochwang'i, Caleb Robinson, Girmaw A. Tadesse, Gilles Q. Hacheme, Rahul Dodhia, Inbal Becker-Reshef, Juan M. Lavista Ferres

  • Keywords: security
  • Abstract: Government agencies use crop maps to estimate production, monitor food security, and set agricultural policy. These crop maps are usually validated against field labels, but these labels are rarely a random sample of the area the map covers. Field teams tend to label parcels near roads and skip ones that are small or hard to reach, so performance measured on held-out labels mainly describes the places they visited. We call the difference between this reported performance and performance over the whole mapped region the deployment performance gap. To estimate performance over the whole region, we use a weighted form of prediction-powered inference (PPI). The classifier's predictions on unlabeled points across the region give a first estimate, and its errors on the labeled points correct that estimate, with more weight on labels from under-sampled conditions. We test this in five European regions where crop registers label every declared parcel, so the true performance is known. Simulating six documented collection practices there produces gaps of up to 0.20 macro-F1. On the collection rule that creates the largest gap in each region, 5 km block cross-validation still overestimates macro-F1 by as much as 0.18, while PPI with weights estimated from satellite embeddings reduces the largest error across regions to 0.07. Importance-weighted cross-validation with the same weights does almost as well, so most of the improvement comes from the weights themselves. We also apply the method to three Kenyan datasets that have no region-wide reference labels. For a roadside dataset collected with helmet-mounted cameras, the estimated macro-F1 falls from 0.93 under random cross-validation to 0.61, although the correction relies on a small number of heavily weighted labels. Our code is available at this https URL.

  • Abstract: We study uniform language generation under approximate differential privacy.   • Abstract: Multi-object image generation in text-to-image diffusion models remains challenging, as existing approaches struggle to reliably generate all objects specified in a prompt. In this work, we propose a training-free framework, termed conditional object composition, that relaxes joint generation methods by taking one step back toward compositional approaches. Segmenters should therefore be evaluated on four axes: temporal agreement, reference recovery, clip recognition, and sequence error, rather than on overlap alone.

Administrative actions use a separate authorization path. We specify the protocol's trust assumptions and give conditional arguments for intent preservation, replay resistance, and a fixed-epoch exposure bound. A Python reference monitor authenticates synthetic records with Ed25519 and serializes reservations with SQLite. It rejects 1,200 negative requests, accepts 3,000 valid synthetic requests, and preserves a 100-unit budget under 64 concurrent attempts in each of 20 trials. When the intent authority is deliberately compromised, malicious requests pass, but the independent budget still limits admitted exposure. The study evaluates a normalized policy model, not Binance's infrastructure or a deployed custody platform. Its contribution is an explicit authorization boundary and reproducible tests of that boundary.

Title: ShiftRAG: Bypassing the Textual Bottleneck via Decoupled Learning and Continuous Soft Tokens

  • Authors: Youmin Ko, Jihong Jeong, Hyunjoon Kim
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00871
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00871
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00871]] ShiftRAG: Bypassing the Textual Bottleneck via Decoupled Learning and Continuous Soft Tokens(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00871)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Despite advances in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), suppressing hallucinations during complex reasoning remains a persistent challenge. We identify that existing RAG pipelines suffer from a textual bottleneck: by passing only raw text to the generator, they discard discriminative signals of the retriever. Furthermore, recent attempts to unify retrieval and generation within a single LLM induce severe objective conflict and exacerbate the rank collapse of decoder-only architectures, degrading performance on both tasks. Concurrently, our soft orthogonality objective mitigates rank collapse, while layer-wise relevance dynamics preserve the fine-grained latent space required for highly discriminative evidence separation. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that ShiftRAG significantly improves retrieval and question answering performance over state-of-the-art baselines. ShiftRAG preserves foundational capabilities of the backbone while maintaining a favorable efficiency-performance trade-off on general text encoding and generative tasks.

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00875

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: The Transformer architecture plays a central role in modern deep learning, and its SE(3)-equivariant variants have been widely applied to molecular conformation modeling and optimization. Given its importance, many methods attempt to interpret and improve the Transformer through the lens of dynamics. However, they mainly focus on a single attention head rather than the full Transformer architecture and seldom consider SE(3)-equivariance in their proposed models. In this study, we propose an improved SE(3)-Transformer architecture, called HamFormer, which revisits the design of SE(3)-Transformer through the lens of Hamiltonian flow. Title: What Breaks and What Comes Back: Capability Damage and Recovery in Depth-Pruned Small Language Models

  • Authors: Piyush Dhoka, Vedant Pandhare, Varun Inamdar, Aadarsh Pathre, Pavitha Nooji
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG

  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Depth pruning removes whole Transformer blocks to make a language model smaller and faster, but the damage is not spread evenly across what the model can do, and a short fine-tuning run, called healing, decides what comes back. We measure this for small models used as agents. Four instruction-tuned models of 1.2-1.7B parameters (Llama-3.2-1B, Qwen2.5-1.5B, SmolLM2-1.7B and OLMo-2-1B) are pruned and healed, and six capabilities are tracked: structured output, function calling, instruction following, mathematical reasoning, safety refusal and knowledge. Third, we test a planner that allocates a 1M-token healing budget from 21 short pilot heals. At a fixed budget it raises the weakest capability on two families, although only one of these gains survives a test that also resamples training seeds. Once its 3.48M pilot tokens are counted, uniform healing with the same total number of training tokens matches or beats every planned variant on every family and retains math significantly better on three of them in an item-level test (on one when seeds are also resampled). Healing longer is a strong and simple baseline that planned recovery must beat. Code, per-item predictions and every result are public.

  • Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training has become an important paradigm for improving multimodal large language models (MLLMs), especially with automatically verifiable rewards. However, existing methods mainly exploit within-instance structure, offering limited supervision for cross-instance discrimination and fine-grained comparison. We introduce Group-and-Order Self-Supervised Reinforcement Learning (GO-SSL), a cross-instance jigsaw framework for vision-centric MLLM post-training. Further evaluations across model scales and families support its generality. Title: ZOPGA-KD: Zeroth Order PGA Guided Data Free Knowledge Distillation

  • Authors: Tuhin Subhra Mukherjee, Tiya Rose Pulikunnel, Vaidhav Adit, Devanand Thanissery, S.H. Shabbeer Basha
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00908
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.00908
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.00908]] ZOPGA-KD: Zeroth Order PGA Guided Data Free Knowledge Distillation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00908)
  • Keywords: privacy, data-free, generative
  • Abstract: Knowledge distillation (KD) enables the transfer of learned representations from a large teacher network to a small student network. In foundational KD, the student network uses the original training data of the teacher model to accomplish this task. However, due to privacy concerns, the original training data is often not available. To overcome this reliance on original training data, several data-free knowledge distillation methods have been developed. Some of these methods operate in the white-box setting, and a few methods generate synthetic data by training a generator network. White-box methods use the teacher model's parameters to obtain a synthetic dataset. To avoid relying on teacher parameters, some methods use computationally expensive generative approaches to obtain such a dataset. There also exist certain black-box methods that generate the synthetic data without accessing teacher parameters. These methods are also computationally intensive. We propose a data-free knowledge distillation method that utilizes just the teacher's output logits and does not require access to its architecture, parameters, or gradients. We generate synthetic data via Projected Gradient Ascent (PGA) starting from smooth random noise signals. Required gradients are obtained through zeroth-order gradient estimation rather than backpropagating through the teacher's parameters. The student is then trained on these synthetically generated samples. We evaluate the proposed method on several benchmark datasets and show that the distillation performance is comparable with approaches that have access to the teacher model parameters.

  • Keywords: defense, attack, steal, large language model
  • Abstract: The communication topology of Large Language Model Multi-Agent Systems (LLM-MASs) governs how information propagates among agents and encodes critical domain knowledge and expert workflow design. Existing defenses detect or mitigate such attacks but do not proactively conceal the topology itself. We propose \textit{Illusion}, a proactive topology obfuscation defense for LLM-MAS. \textit{Illusion} injects fake edges and fake messages to present attackers with a deceptive topology, while selecting low-impact candidates that preserve output-distribution equivalence and task-accuracy non-inferiority. It also largely preserves output distributions and task performance, and its fake edges resist both similarity-based and LLM-judge filtering, even under adaptive pruning.

  • Abstract: Early-exit language models decide for each token how much computation to spend, usually by stopping once the prediction is confident. In Qwen3, along two adaptive axes (the timesteps of a spiking conversion and the layers of the original Transformer), confidence stopping gives the continuation pieces of a word less computation than its first piece in English, German, and Turkish, but more in Bangla. A learned stopping head beats confidence stopping by 7 to 8 points, but gains less than one point over the best mix of fixed exits at equal cost, and sometimes loses.   • Authors: Amin Najafgholizadeh, Mohsen Afsharchi
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.GR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01048
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01048
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01048]] Infinite Worlds from One Example: What Local Generators Must Share(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01048)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Seeded noise lets games generate any region of an unbounded world on demand. We ask what such a generator needs when every small window must come from one example, the constraint behind Wave Function Collapse. The difficulty is that local constraints can require agreement over arbitrary distances: in a brick wall, each row keeps its place between the mortar lines. We study local generators, each cell a fixed function of the randomness near it. When the constraints carry a quantity that varies across the example, no generator that applies one rule, ignoring position, to independent randomness at every cell can be both exactly valid and faithful to the example's statistics, however far each cell reads. At radius r, staying faithful forces forbidden windows at a density of at least order 1/r, and a construction attains this order. To vary the quantity between exactly valid worlds, a generator needs randomness shared along its direction. We give a finitely checkable condition under which row types drawn from the seed and the position modulo a fixed period suffice for exact validity without learning. A search over the example's patches certifies these quantities and names the input each needs. Across 52 public examples of Wave Function Collapse and 27 from other implementations, it certifies only examples where a learned generator on independent randomness stays far from valid after repair. Given that input, a neural cellular automaton and a diffusion model become valid on 10 and 8 of the 12 certified examples that receive one, and on none from independent randomness. Valid means at most one forbidden window in a thousand after repair. With octaves of seeded noise elsewhere, the automaton is valid on 45 of the 52 public examples and 21 of the 27 from other implementations, and repair keeps random access.

Title: Synergetic Autonomous Intelligence: Three Axioms for Recursive Semi-Closed Learning

  • Authors: Dongxu Li
  • Subjects: cs.LG

  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: We introduce Synergetic Autonomous Intelligence (SAI), a theory and design program for learning systems that form, preserve, revise, and compose relations while continuing to learn. SAI is built on three axioms: consequential distinctions must not become orphaned (U); identity is determined by future-faithful continuation under a declared contract (I); and unresolved defects are repaired by the least lawful local completion at the lowest admitted grade (L). We model a model, agent, tool, team, or environment boundary as a recursive semi-closed system with a Kernel-Ring representation, a Compile-Open-Participate cycle, and a typed residual comparing actual with claimed continuation. KREIT, the Kernel-Ring Equilibrium Information Theory layer, treats information as a typed, resource-bounded distinction; the control layer authorizes, executes, audits, and updates actions across cloud-edge-end scales. These results establish a common contract-level vocabulary for representation, relation, memory, action, communication, explanation, coordination, and growth, while marking the assumptions and open boundaries required for later generalization.

Title: Multi-Turn Semantic Caching for Low-Cost LLM Serving

  • Authors: Baran Atalar, Selly Beyene, Xutong Liu, Carlee Joe-Wong
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CL, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01077
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01077
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01077]] Multi-Turn Semantic Caching for Low-Cost LLM Serving(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01077)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Semantic caching lowers the cost of serving large language models (LLMs) by reusing stored responses for similar requests. In multi-turn conversations, whether a cached response is acceptable depends on both conversations' histories, which embedding similarity of requests and histories predicts poorly (AUC 0.62--0.74 on five workloads). We therefore learn the \emph{reuse risk}, the probability that a cached response is unacceptable, from judge feedback, and choose a budgeted set of conversation states to cache by weighing the generation cost a reuse avoids against a price for unacceptable reuse. We propose CUCB-SC-MT, which learns a cache from logged conversations with a suboptimality guarantee, and CLCB-LS-MT, which learns online and has $\tilde O(\sqrt N)$ regret over $N$ requests when conversation states recur. On math-tutoring (SocraTeach) and movie-ticketing (Taskmaster-3) conversations, both achieve lower loss than prior semantic caching approaches: ContextCache, multi-turn variants of ElastiCache and vCache, and single-turn semantic caching at every cache budget. With 1,000 cache entries, CLCB-LS-MT avoids 35--41\% of the generation cost through acceptable reuses, against at most 22--25\% for the baselines, and lowers estimated serving latency by 19--35\%.

Title: CuPH: Parallel Cubical Persistent Homology via Level-Synchronous Component Tracking

  • Authors: Guangyu Meng, Pengfei Gu, Erin Wolf Chambers, Danny Z. Chen
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.CG, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01078
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01078
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01078]] CuPH: Parallel Cubical Persistent Homology via Level-Synchronous Component Tracking(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01078)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Persistent homology (PH) is a mathematically principled multi-scale topological descriptor of shape and connectivity in data. It is widely applied in image classification, segmentation, and shape analysis across medical and aerial imaging, providing structural information that pixel-wise deep learning may overlook. Standard cubical PH decomposes an image into cubical cells (vertices, edges, and squares) and reduces them sequentially in filtration order. This sequential reduction has $\Theta(n)$ parallel depth for an $n$-pixel image, limiting within-image GPU parallelism and constraining its use in topology-aware deep learning. We introduce CuPH, a GPU-based parallel algorithm for computing cubical PH on 2D images that operates directly on pixels rather than cubical cells. CuPH buckets pixels by filtration values, activates them with a level-synchronous sweep, and tracks connected components with a lock-free union-find method. We prove that CuPH produces persistence diagrams identical to standard cubical PH on integer inputs, with a stability bound for floating-point inputs. CuPH reduces parallel depth from $\Theta(n)$ to $O(L\log n)$ for an image with $L$ intensity levels. We implement the level sweep as a single CUDA kernel, eliminating cross-level kernel-launch overhead. Empirically, CuPH is $18.6$--$99.6\times$ faster than the existing GPU baseline (CMP). As a drop-in PH backend, CuPH accelerates topology-aware classification ($4.2$--$16.7\times$) and segmentation ($8.3$--$44\times$) while preserving accuracy. It also enables computing full-image PH losses on high-resolution images, improving segmentation Dice by $2.2$--$4.6$ points over patch-local PH baselines and accelerating training by $25$--$31\times$.

Title: VeilFL: Two-Stage Accountability for Anonymous Federated Learning

  • Authors: Varesh Mishra, Aysajan Abidin, Bart Preneel
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01082
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01082
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01082]] VeilFL: Two-Stage Accountability for Anonymous Federated Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01082)
  • Keywords: security, privacy, federate
  • Abstract: Anonymous credentials can hide which registered client participates in federated learning, but their unlinkability also makes repeated misconduct difficult to associate. We present VeilFL, a two-aggregator protocol that adds staged accountability to PINE (USENIX Security '24). Each submission binds two private PINE messages, a round-specific pseudonym, and a tracing pair to the same hidden credential secret. This design enables detection of duplicate submissions within a round without introducing a persistent identifier for normal participation. VeilFL implements accountability in two stages. After a confirmed duplicate or joint PINE rejection, a threshold tracing committee reveals a stable handle that links faults associated with the same credential. The client's registered identity is recoverable only after the public log records the requisite number of linked faults and a separate threshold committee authorizes identification. Accepted and unopened submissions remain unlinkable under the stated assumptions. This work establishes submission binding and duplicate detection, anonymity, fault non-frameability, controlled two-stage opening, and preservation of PINE's privacy and validity guarantees. In the prototype implementation, creating a submission requires a median of 2.11 ms, while verification requires 3.01 ms. Batch verification reduces the cost to 1.86 ms per submission for a batch of 1,000. The public part of each submission is 608 bytes, with 1,248 bytes of additional VeilFL protocol data across the two aggregator submissions. Each opening stage completes within 4.3 ms for the different committee configurations.

  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01089

  • Abstract: Existing serving systems for large language models batch requests for many different LoRA adapters on one GPU. One solution to this is to convert these adapters to LoRA, but this induces a significant memory and accuracy cost. Instead, we show that, under some modifications, a batched LoRA kernel can serve all of these methods exactly and efficiently. Under a burst of requests on an RTX A6000, a mix of these methods takes 5-17% more decode time than the same number of LoRA adapters. Furthermore, using Scute, the same GPU can serve 64 to 1024 (IA) and VeRA adapters at 1.5x to at least 2.5x the request rate of their LoRA conversions.

Title: The Blind Spots of Detection: Adversarial Perturbation Attacks against Watchdogs in Distributed LLM Inference

  • Authors: Siu Wai Shi, Xiaochen Zhu, Xinjian Luo, Keke Huang, Bingxue Zhang, Xiaofeng Gao
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01097
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01097
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01097]] The Blind Spots of Detection: Adversarial Perturbation Attacks against Watchdogs in Distributed LLM Inference(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01097)
  • Keywords: security, defense, attack, robust, steal, large language model
  • Abstract: Decentralizing large language model (LLM) inference across untrusted Web nodes requires robust verification to ensure computational integrity. Existing activation-based verification and detection mechanisms validate intermediate outputs against reference values, permitting bounded numerical deviations to accommodate benign cross-hardware discrepancies. However, a critical security question remains: what can a malicious worker achieve while operating within these tolerance bounds? In this work, we introduce Hush-Up, a stealthy availability attack against distributed LLM inference, where a single malicious worker halts generation while evading attribution. The attacker first profiles its local execution to model an empirical bound of benign hardware errors. Using this error bound as a feasibility constraint, the attacker optimizes an adversarial perturbation on its output activations to maximize the final-layer probability of the end-of-sequence (EOS) token, i.e., to hush up the model. Across diverse models and benchmarks, Hush-Up effectively forces premature generation termination and collapses model capabilities. Furthermore, the attack hushes up the watchdogs themselves: existing defenses often fail to detect the sabotage entirely or falsely attribute the amplified downstream anomalies to honest workers. Our findings expose a fundamental gap between local numerical verification and end-to-end accountability: perturbations too small to indict a worker locally can nevertheless be powerful enough to break distributed inference globally.

This paper addresses catastrophic forgetting in task-free federated forecasting under concept drift. Forward, from a code change: a change to authentication middleware scopes to its governing domain slice and returns the obligations the change must satisfy. Inverse, from a normative obligation: "make this NIS2-compliant" resolves a generic legal duty into the concrete engineering obligations it implies across slices, surfacing precisely the fraction of a mostly-governance regulation that lands in code. The same working set is what a human engineer reads and what an LLM-grounded assistant is handed as grounding. The use turns "read every applicable authority" into "satisfy this typed working set", for a code change or a compliance duty alike. The contribution is the demonstrated use; the artefact, its validation, and its governance are companion work.

Title: Rethinking LLM Pruning via Test-Time Scaling

  • Authors: Mingyang Song, Mao Zheng
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01133
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01133
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01133]] Rethinking LLM Pruning via Test-Time Scaling(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01133)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Structured pruning reduces the size of large language models, often at the cost of lower accuracy. Whether this decline reflects diminished reasoning capacity or greater difficulty in sampling correct solutions is less clear. We examine this question across expert, FFN and layer pruning on four mathematical and coding benchmarks, comparing original and pruned models through pass@k at matched sampling budgets. Even after substantial pruning, some models match or exceed the original model's reasoning coverage despite lower pass@1, and solve problems left unsolved in the original samples. Retaining broad coverage, however, does not mean that correct solutions remain easy to obtain. Under severe pruning, diverse generated chain-of-thought (CoT) traces can coexist with increased repetition and less reliable generation. As correct answers become harder to sample, the additional attempts can sharply increase total token use even when individual responses become shorter. This separation between coverage and sampling efficiency also clarifies expert-selection gains, as methods with markedly different pass@1 can solve the same problems at large budgets yet require different numbers of attempts to reach a common coverage target. Pruning should therefore be assessed by both the range of problems a model can still solve and the sampling cost of obtaining correct solutions.

Title: Enhancing Edge AI System Security with a Stacked Hardware Security Controller for Robust and Energy-Efficient Anomaly Detectio

  • Authors: Carl Benda, Eren Kurshan, Paul Franzon
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.ET
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01150
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01150
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01150]] Enhancing Edge AI System Security with a Stacked Hardware Security Controller for Robust and Energy-Efficient Anomaly Detectio(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01150)
  • Keywords: security, robust
  • Abstract: In recent years, the growing adoption of Edge AI systems has been accompanied by a surge in security threats. Edge AI deployments face an rapidly evolving spectrum of security threats, from side-channel information leakage to ad- versarial model manipulation and physical device tampering. Conventional security mechanisms such as malware scanners and resource-intensive firewall solutions are unsuitable due to edge AIs typically limited computational resources, strict energy budgets and thermal constraints especially in battery-powered and field-deployed environments. This paper proposes a stacked, hardware-based security mechanism that enables low-overhead, continuous detection of anomalous execution patterns. Anomaly detection utilizing hard- ware performance counters and physical sensors outperforms software-based alternatives by providing key capabilities such as tamper-resistant root-of-trust isolation, negligible runtime overhead, and resilience against compromised operating systems. Experimental analysis shows that adversarial or malicious ap- plications produce observable deviations in sensor and thermal readings on Edge AI systems and hardware-based anomaly detection provides effective and low-overhead anomaly detection.

Title: SHield: Watermarking 4D Gaussian Splatting via Spherical Harmonic Coefficient Perturbation

  • Authors: Kunal Bhosikar, Prajas Wadekar, Venkata Sai Pranav Bachina, Ankit Gangwal, Charu Sharma, Nicole Meng, Yingjie Lao
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.CR, cs.GR, cs.MM
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01162
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01162
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01162]] SHield: Watermarking 4D Gaussian Splatting via Spherical Harmonic Coefficient Perturbation(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01162)
  • Keywords: protect, robust, watermark
  • Abstract: 4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS) models are computationally expensive to train but distributed as easily copied parameter files. Existing watermarks protect these assets by fine-tuning the scene against a neural decoder to extract bit strings from rendered video, a heavy process vulnerable to the temporal dynamics of dynamic scenes. However, if the threat model targets the redistributed file itself, verification can be vastly simplified. Because the canonical Spherical-Harmonic (SH) coefficients of 4DGS are time- and view-invariant, parameter-space perturbations can be recovered via deterministic arithmetic. We propose **SHield**, the first non-blind, decoder-free watermarking approach that natively embeds full-resolution RGB images into 4DGS files. SHield tiles a zero-mean payload across the SH coefficients of a key-selected Gaussian subset, applying a brief anchored optimization to preserve visual fidelity. To verify ownership, it geometrically re-associates the key using canonical Gaussian centres to survive file tampering and recovers the payload in closed form. Across 30 scenes (D-NeRF, HyperNeRF, DyNeRF), SHield guarantees near-perfect payload recovery (Pearson $r \geq 0.991$) on all 150 tested models with a mean rendering penalty of $\leq 0.13$~dB. Held-out evaluation yields perfect detection on 400 true trials and zero false positives across $1{,}600$ wrong-subset null trials. We also strictly bound the method's robustness: SHield survives INT8 quantization and $30\%$ opacity pruning for the image payload, and on aggressive SH-degree-1 truncation bit payloads are still decodable.

Title: Lightweight Representation Learning for Low Tucker Rank Tensors

  • Authors: Hiroki Hasegawa, Yukihiko Okada
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01168
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01168
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01168]] Lightweight Representation Learning for Low Tucker Rank Tensors(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01168)
  • Keywords: extraction
  • Abstract: Low Tucker rank tensors provide compact representations of multidimensional dependencies, but standard learning pipelines can incur substantial computational and memory overhead from redundant intermediate quantities. We reduce this overhead without modifying the underlying representation objective or parameterization. Our approach combines three complementary components: (i) sequential extraction of only task-required spectral components, avoiding unnecessary full decompositions; (ii) factor-wise multilinear contractions without materializing large Kronecker operators; and (iii) compact latent-state updates without storing full ambient tensor iterates. These components preserve the algebraic operations and learning trajectories of the original algorithms while reducing their computational and memory footprints. Across five low Tucker rank tensor estimators, our implementations match the original trajectories to numerical precision (relative discrepancies of $10^{-16}$ to $10^{-13}$), while reducing per-iteration runtime by up to $3.12\times$ and resident memory by up to $616\times$ ($99.8\%$) on the main order-3 benchmark at $n=100$, with memory reductions exceeding $900\times$ across the full evaluation suite. These results show that substantial computational redundancy can be eliminated from Tucker representation learning without changing the underlying model or learned representation.

Title: Fast and Exact Relative-Position Attention via Structure-Aware Kernels

  • Authors: Delyan Boychev, Ivo Petrov, Dimitar I. Dimitrov
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.DC
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01182
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01182
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01182]] Fast and Exact Relative-Position Attention via Structure-Aware Kernels(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01182)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Relative-position attention mechanisms offer compelling alternatives to RoPE across various transformer architectures, delivering superior performance in domains such as audio. These mechanisms typically modify attention scores by adding content-position or bias-position terms. Unlike RoPE, which encodes position via per-token rotations directly on the queries and keys, relative-position terms generally require pairwise offset-dependent computation or lookups, complicating their integration into fused attention kernels and introducing severe memory, bandwidth, and compute overheads as sequences scale. To address this bottleneck, we propose an exact attention algorithm that incorporates additive relative-position terms into FlashAttention-style tiled computation. Our method (1) uses offset-aware tiling to avoid redundant indexing and memory accesses, (2) computes only the necessary key/query-relation products and reuses them across token pairs in both forward and backward passes, and (3) uses offset-based gradient sharding and aggregation, eliminating atomic contention. We provide a unified, efficient Triton implementation across diverse relative-position attention mechanisms and evaluate it against naive and specialized implementations. For 8K-token sequences, our method speeds up the forward (backward) pass by 1.8-6.4$\times$ (2.0-21$\times$) and reduces peak memory up to 2.9-128$\times$ (1.4-54$\times$) over the strongest baseline for each mechanism, enabling long-context end-to-end transcription that is 1.8$\times$ faster and uses 2.4$\times$ less memory. Our method thus provides a drop-in, hardware-efficient solution for scaling relative-position attention transformers.

Title: A Large-Scale Evaluation of Cookie Notice Privacy and Accessibility for Blind and Low Vision Users

  • Authors: James M. Clarke, Maryam Mehrnezhad
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01211
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01211
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01211]] A Large-Scale Evaluation of Cookie Notice Privacy and Accessibility for Blind and Low Vision Users(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01211)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: We present the first large-scale privacy and accessibility evaluation of cookie notices across 2,061 domains for blind and low vision (BLV) users. Using a Playwright-based crawler, a custom NVDA screen reader add-on, and the Lighthouse and WAVE accessibility tools, we measure UK GDPR/PECR compliance, tracker activity across pre-interaction, post-accept, and post-reject phases, and cookie notice accessibility for screen reader users. We find that 51 % of sites display a cookie notice, of which (at least) 50 % violate UK GDPR/PECR requirements. Accepting a notice produces a mean five-fold increase in tracker activity, and degrades page landmark structure on one in five sites. While 95 % of notices are readable by a screen reader, only 37 % carry a meaningful title, 39 % are announced immediately on page load, and 42 % use descriptive button labels. We reveal a systemic correlation: cookie notices failing legal privacy requirements disproportionately exhibit the weakest screen reader accessibility. This compounding privacy burden on BLV users demands urgent intervention from developers, regulators, and standards bodies.

Title: Optimal Conversion from Zero-Concentrated to Gaussian Differential Privacy

  • Authors: Yu-Wei Chen, Aaradhya Pandey, Jordan Awan
  • Subjects: cs.CR, math.ST
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01218
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01218
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01218]] Optimal Conversion from Zero-Concentrated to Gaussian Differential Privacy(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01218)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: Gaussian differential privacy (GDP) and zero-concentrated differential privacy (zCDP) are two competing privacy accounting frameworks, but they share similar minimax lower bounds in various estimation problems. Moreover, it is known that a mechanism satisfying $\mu$-GDP satisfies $(\mu^2/2)$-zCDP, whereas the converse fails to hold at the same calibration. In this paper, we establish the optimal reverse conversion, showing that every $\rho$-zCDP mechanism satisfies $\mu_{\mathrm{zCDP}}(\rho)$-GDP, where $\mu_{\mathrm{zCDP}}(\rho)=2\Phi^{-1}((1+e^{-t_\rho})^{-1})$ and $t_\rho$ uniquely solves the equation $\rho=t_\rho\tanh(t_\rho/2)$. We also obtain simplified bounds $\sqrt{\pi\rho}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{zCDP}}(\rho)\leq\sqrt{8\rho}$, with sharp constants, which imply equivalence of the two frameworks up to constants. This explains why their minimax risks agree up to a constant rescaling of the privacy budget. We show that the squared conversion is strictly convex, which implies that converting zCDP to GDP before applying composition or group privacy yields a tighter GDP guarantee than converting afterward. We also derive the sharp conversion from bounded-range and logistic DP mechanisms (e.g., exponential mechanism) to GDP, where converting before composition but after group privacy gives the tighter guarantee.

Title: The Brittle Compass: Navigating LLM Prompt Sensitivity in Slovak Migration Media Discourse

  • Keywords: robust, extraction
  • Abstract: In this work, we present a case study that explores various tasks centered around the topic of migration in Slovak, a low-resource language, such as topic relevance and geographical relevance classification, and migration source/destination location term extraction. Our results demonstrate that native (Slovak) prompts yield a modest, task-dependent gains, while large models show significant robustness to prompt variations compared to their smaller counterparts. Analysis reveals that instructions (system or task) emerge as the most critical prompt component, more so than the examples sections, with task-specific performance benefits being more pronounced than overall language effects.

Title: Modeling Fat-shift for Improved Head Segmentation from MRI Scans using a Probabilistic Model

  • Authors: Merle Diedrichsen, Koen Van Leemput, Douglas N. Greve, Oula Puonti, Axel Thielscher
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01222
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01222
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01222]] Modeling Fat-shift for Improved Head Segmentation from MRI Scans using a Probabilistic Model(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01222)
  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Personalized computational head models for biophysical simulations rely on accurate whole-head segmentations from T1-weighted (T1w) magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) scans. While T1w scans provide excellent contrast for intracranial anatomy, extracerebral tissues have partly almost no signal (compact bone) and also suffer from the so-called chemical shift artifact, which affects the fatty tissues in MRI and causes them to spatially shift in the frequency encoding direction of the scan. This artifact occurs primarily in two structures of the head: the spongy bone, a fatty compartment inside the compact bone, and the subcutaneous fat, located directly underneath the skin. The frequency-encoding direction is often set in the superior-inferior direction, causing the fatty tissues to shift down and, as a result, making the skull appear thinner than it actually is. Existing segmentation methods do not model this artifact, consequently leading to inaccurate labeling of the skull and scalp, which introduces a bias in biophysical simulations. The direction and magnitude of the fat-shift can be determined from the scan parameters, thus making it possible to explicitly model the shift and to recover the correct anatomy. This study introduces a probabilistic model that uses variational inference to segment head and brain structures from a T1w scan while accounting for the fat-shift artifact. The proposed Fat-shift-informed Label Optimization of the Whole head (FLOW) method reconstructs extracerebral anatomy more accurately than a fat-shift-agnostic segmentation approach while maintaining high segmentation accuracy in all other structures. FLOW-based fat-shift corrected segmentations significantly reduce bias in electric field simulations compared to a segmentation method that does not model the shift.

Title: Mathematical Formulation and Comparative Performance Evaluation of Machine Learning Algorithms for Maize Land Suitability Prediction

  • Authors: Haimanot Bitew, Ayisheshim Almaw, Habtamu Zegeye, Demeke Alene
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01239

  • Keywords: extraction
  • Abstract: Addressing the food needs of a growing population is one of the major challenges of developing countries like Ethiopia. Various policies aimed at increasing food production have been enacted by governments and development partners. Although efforts in this regard have been made, there is still inadequate emphasis on increasing food production through the use of high technology practices. Precision agriculture using technology has been utilized to increase crop yield. Precision agriculture also relies on identifying land for a crop, which is a key factor that affects crop yield. Traditional methods of assessing land suitability for crop production are time-consuming, costly and unreliable. Against this background, the aim of this study was to assess the potential of machine learning (ML) in improving the speed and accuracy of assessing land suitability for maize, a staple crop in Ethiopia, as compared to traditional methods. The study is investigated in three areas (Burie, Shindi and Tilili) in the Amhara region. Data collection was done through the local agriculture offices and survey teams. The data were coded, scaled and balanced. Various feature extraction techniques, which included Univariate Feature Selection(UFS), Recursive Feature Elimination with Cross-Validation(RFECV) and Sequential Feature Selection(SFS), and various classifiers, which were K-nearest neighbor (KNN), gradient boosting (GB),random forest (RF) with mathematical formulations were employed. The best models were constructed through combining GBOOST and SFS and achieved an accuracy of 99.41% and 99.35% for both precision and recall. Our results showed that using machine learning is a reliable method of assessing land suitability for maize in Ethiopia. Keywords: Maize, Gradient boosting,Land suitability,Machine learning, Mathematical formulations

Title: Context-Shielded Backdoor Attack against Large Vision-Language Models

  • Authors: Zhifang Zhang, Qiang Huang, Chenpeng Wang, Weitong Chen, Olaf Maennel, Wei Emma Zhang, Lei Feng, Lei Zhang, Miao Xu
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01252
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01252
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01252]] Context-Shielded Backdoor Attack against Large Vision-Language Models(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01252)
  • Keywords: attack
  • Abstract: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) routinely use inference-time visual-token compression to prune hundreds of patch tokens down to a compact budget. Existing LVLM backdoors fire whenever a triggered image appears, whereas prior deployment-triggered attacks activate only when model weights are quantized or pruned. However, conventional LVLM backdoors exhibit nearly 100% attack success rate (ASR) under full-token inference, immediately exposing compromised checkpoints during pre-deployment auditing. We reveal context stripping: surrounding clean visual tokens suppress an embedded trigger at full length, whereas token pruning strips this context away and activates the backdoor. Motivated by this finding, we propose StripWake, a projector-only backdoor that remains dormant under full-token auditing yet activates across unseen compressors. Specifically, StripWake pairs clean-supervised full-token views with target-supervised randomized compressed views, and aligns early visual attention with a frozen benign teacher to preserve clean utility. On LLaVA-1.5-7B across nine unseen compressors, StripWake stays dormant at 0.4% full-token ASR yet achieves 94.0%-99.7% compressed ASR across five primary compressors without CIDEr loss.

Title: EZ-Ar-VC: Robust Arabic Voice Anonymization with Generalization across Dialects and Code-Switching

  • Authors: Injy Hamed, Adarsh Arigala, Rufael Marew, Arjun Gangwar, Atharva Kulkarni, Hanan Aldarmaki, Srinivasan Umesh
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01268
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01268
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01268]] EZ-Ar-VC: Robust Arabic Voice Anonymization with Generalization across Dialects and Code-Switching(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01268)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Voice conversion (VC) models can be used to anonymize speech data while preserving naturalness. Existing models, however, are mainly optimized for English and other high-resource languages, and they fail to generalize well to Arabic due to its distinct phonology. In this work, we comprehensively evaluate existing zero-shot VC systems for Arabic speech, identifying limitations in pronunciation preservation. We then train an Arabic-centric model based on EZ-VC on diverse Arabic speech, and demonstrate strong performance in intelligibility, naturalness, and pronunciation preservation, while maintaining high speaker dissimilarity. Through subjective evaluation, we further demonstrate its robustness to code-switching and cross-dialectal conversion, as well as its generalization to Arabic dialects with limited representation in the training data. Our findings highlight the limitations of zero-shot VC and the importance of target-language adaptation for robust voice anonymization.

Title: DriftingPlanner: Drifting Generative Models for One-Step Trajectory Planning in End-to-End Autonomous Driving

  • Authors: Ziming Liu, Yizhan Qu, Leichen Wang, Hao Sun
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.RO
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01283
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01283
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01283]] DriftingPlanner: Drifting Generative Models for One-Step Trajectory Planning in End-to-End Autonomous Driving(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01283)
  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: End-to-end planners decode the ego trajectory either by coordinate regression, which is fast but blends plausible maneuvers into one trajectory that may follow none of them, or by diffusion and flow matching, which model the trajectory distribution but need several inference steps. Drifting models move this iteration into training: a one-step generator learns to follow an attraction--repulsion field between generated and ground-truth samples. Drifting was developed for class-conditional image generation, where every class supplies many positive samples. In planning, every scene is its own condition. Pooling scenes would attract the generated trajectories of one scene to the ground truth of unrelated scenes, whereas computing the field per scene leaves a single positive, the expert trajectory. Mutual repulsion then holds the generated trajectories on a shell around it, so any single generated trajectory deviates from the ground truth. We present DriftingPlanner, a trajectory planner trained with the drifting loss alone, without any coordinate regression term. It computes the field per scene and removes the shell at inference: it decodes every noise vector together with its negation in one batched pass, and averaging each antithetic pair cancels the linear part of the displacement from the shell center. On NAVSIM navtest, DriftingPlanner reaches a state-of-the-art 93.75 PDMS, and with single-step inference its drifting decoder comes within 0.13 PDMS of the best diffusion or flow matching setting, which needs ten inference steps.

Title: Character-Name Concentration in LLM Fiction: Measuring Displacement and Mitigating Repetition

  • Authors: Alejandro Mozo Quesada (1), Sergio Gálvez Rojas (1), Francisco López Valverde (1) ((1) Universidad de Málaga, Spain)
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01284
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01284
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01284]] Character-Name Concentration in LLM Fiction: Measuring Displacement and Mitigating Repetition(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01284)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Character names in large language model (LLM) fiction concentrate on a handful of recurrent names such as 'Elara' and 'Elias', a measurable face of AI slop. Existing fixes either require logit access or retraining, or operate per request and cannot control concentration across a corpus. How much prompt-level strategies reduce concentration, and at what cost to name naturalness, has not been systematically measured. We present an empirical study of name-concentration control across eight strategies and introduce speculative naming, a fine-tuning-free method that adapts the draft-verify-reject paradigm of speculative decoding from tokens to names. The method requests candidate name-sets through verbalized sampling, scores them against a SlopTracker that combines a per-model fingerprint with a runtime counter, and selects through a temperature-controlled rule or its strict variant (HARD). We use 20 LLMs to generate 44,782 stories in four languages from text and visual stimuli (88,919 named characters), and measure concentration with a list-free collision metric (Slop Ratio, SR) decomposed into known and emergent components. List-based strategies do not remove slop: static banlists displace concentration to emergent names and more than halve the share of attested real names. Speculative naming removes it: the sampling scaffold alone cuts SR by 89% on average, the dynamic tracker brings the total reduction to 95%, mean collision probability falls from 7.3% to 0.4%, and known-name collisions hold at zero while naturalness stays close to baseline (42-69% attested by language) and within-story collisions drop from 4.3% to 0.2%. The strategy ranking is stable across languages, model families, and name-counting conventions. Name concentration is thus controllable at the corpus level without logit access or retraining; broader creative diversity beyond naming remains open.

Title: [Re] Pure Noise to the Rescue of Insufficient Data

  • Authors: Seungjae Ryan Lee, Seungmin Brian Lee
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01295
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01295
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01295]] [Re] Pure Noise to the Rescue of Insufficient Data(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01295)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: We examine the main claims of Zada, Benou, and Irani (2021), which states that in an image classification task with imbalanced training data, (i) using pure noise to augment minority-class images encourages generalization by improving minority-class accuracy. This method is paired with (ii) a new batch normalization layer that normalizes noise images using affine parameters learned from natural images, which improves the model's performance. Moreover, (iii) this improvement is robust to varying levels of data augmentation. Finally, the authors propose that (iv) adding pure noise images can improve classification even on balanced training data. We implemented the training pipeline from the description of the paper using PyTorch and integrated authors' code snippets for sampling pure noise images and batch normalizing noise and natural images separately. We reproduced the main claims that (i) oversampling with pure noise improves generalization by improving the minority-class accuracy, (ii) the proposed batch normalization (BN) method outperforms baselines, (iii) and this improvement is robust across data augmentations. Our results also support that (iv) adding pure noise images can improve classification on balanced training data. However, additional experiments suggest that the performance improvement from OPeN may be more orthogonal to the improvement caused by a bigger network or more complex data augmentation.

Title: Accelerating posterior sampling in Bayesian inverse problems with diffusion models: an online-offline perspective

  • Authors: Duc-Lam Duong
  • Subjects: cs.LG, math.NA, math.ST, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01300
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01300
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01300]] Accelerating posterior sampling in Bayesian inverse problems with diffusion models: an online-offline perspective(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01300)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Bayesian inverse problems require both informative prior models and computationally feasible posterior inference. Score-based diffusion models provide expressive learned priors, but their use for inversion introduces a conditioning problem: the score of a noised posterior is generally unavailable from a prior score alone. This selective survey reviews the principal approaches to that problem, including likelihood guidance, spectral and projection methods, sequential Monte Carlo corrections, and amortized conditional training. We organize methods by how they split cost between offline preparation and online inference, and compare their assumptions, advantages and limitations, with emphasis on computational speedups. Some ongoing directions and open questions are gathered at the end.

Title: CuriousTogether: Implications of Curiosity and Cultural Grounding in Multi-Agent LLM Interactions

  • Authors: Angana Borah, Zoha Hayat Bhatti, Zhijing Jin, Rada Mihalcea
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01301
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01301
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01301]] CuriousTogether: Implications of Curiosity and Cultural Grounding in Multi-Agent LLM Interactions(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01301)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in multi-agent settings, exhibiting emergent collective behaviors, yet the role of curiosity as a driver of such dynamics remains understudied. We introduce CuriousTogether, a framework evaluating curiosity-driven multi-agent LLM interactions across three complementary dimensions: novelty-seeking, epistemic and social curiosity. Across eight tasks spanning reasoning, creative generation, and social bias mitigation, curiosity-guided multi-agent settings yield improvements over non-curious ones on seven out of eight tasks, with gains of up to 20.6% in story generation (semantic diversity) and a 14.9 pp reduction in stereotype rates. Furthermore, country-conditioned cultural grounding show benefits for story generation and social tasks, but can hurt precision-oriented reasoning. Finally, we show that inference-time multi-agent curiosity-based approach offers favorable quality-token trade-offs, compared with single-agent baselines. Together, these findings support curiosity-guided interaction as a practical mechanism for improving multi-agent collaboration, with downstream benefits for reasoning, creative, and societal tasks.

Title: AgentCBO: Causal Belief Routing for Bayesian Optimization under Unknown Graphs

  • Authors: Qiyu Wei, Xin Zhang, Richard Allmendinger, Mauricio A Álvarez
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01317
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01317
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01317]] AgentCBO: Causal Belief Routing for Bayesian Optimization under Unknown Graphs(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01317)
  • Keywords: protect
  • Abstract: Causal Bayesian optimization (CBO) typically relies on the unrealistic assumption of a perfectly known causal graph. Recent methods relax this constraint by coupling optimization with structure learning, but they often waste sample budgets on structural uncertainties that are irrelevant to the optimizer. In this paper, we formalize routing-equivalence to shift the objective from full causal-graph recovery to causal belief routing, where structural differences only matter when they alter optimizer-facing objects such as admissible exploration sets and do-priors. Furthermore, we propose agentcbo, a graph-belief routing framework that incorporates LLM causal priors through guarded proposal and commit mechanisms. Specifically, a Language-Prior Agent combines LLM beliefs with constraint-based discovery to extract a protected causal backbone alongside residual uncertain edges. A Calibration Agent and a Residual Repair Agent then refine only these uncertain structures through score-gated bounded local edits. Finally, a deterministic commit gate evaluates candidate updates using routing-proxy checks before allowing them to influence intervention selection. Across ten benchmarks, agentcbo performs comparably to known-graph CBO, demonstrating that guarded LLM priors can improve fixed-budget CBO performance.

Title: Inferring missing locations in human mobility data using memory effects

  • Authors: Pablo Rosillo-Rodes, Antònia Tugores, David Sánchez, José J. Ramasco
  • Subjects: cs.LG, physics.soc-ph
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01319
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01319
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01319]] Inferring missing locations in human mobility data using memory effects(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01319)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Empirical datasets used to characterize human mobility are sparse in space and time, owing to technological factors such as coverage and battery, or to the erratic nature of online interactions. Missing data can bias the conclusions drawn from them, so filling the gaps in incomplete timelines is a key challenge in computational social science. Here, we propose a simple imputation method in which the informativeness of an observation decays with its bidirectional temporal distance from the missing entry, at a rate set by a single parameter per individual. On synthetic and real Twitter data, a power-law decay, selected by Bayesian model comparison, yields accurate imputations (macro-AUROC above 0.9 overall, and above 0.8 for the most mobile users) and matches a gap-aware continuous-time Markov chain, outperforming it among the most mobile users. The method is robust to moderate noise and generalizes across countries and European administrative regions.

Title: Step-dLLM: Adaptive Step-aware Sparse Attention for Efficient Diffusion LLM Inference

  • Authors: Zhichen Zeng, Xichong Zhang, Junpan Wu, Yifei Zuo, Chi-Chih Chang, Jiayi Wang, Maohua Nie, Ji Liu, Ang Li, Banghua Zhu
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.AI

  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01320]] Step-dLLM: Adaptive Step-aware Sparse Attention for Efficient Diffusion LLM Inference(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01320)
  • Keywords: diffusion, large language model
  • Abstract: Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) generate tokens in parallel via iterative denoising, but their bidirectional attention prevents the KV caching used by autoregressive models and recomputes the full attention map at every step, incurring quadratic per-step cost. Existing sparse attention methods for dLLMs construct a sparse mask after an initial full-attention warm-up and reuse it for the remaining steps, while allocating the sparsity budget uniformly across heads. We argue both choices ignore key structural properties of attention in dLLMs: empirically, attention patterns are only locally consistent across steps and shift abruptly at certain transitions, and different heads concentrate their attention mass to very different extents. To this end, we design Step-dLLM, which periodically refreshes a block-sparse mask at anchor steps along the denoising trajectory and reuses it within each locally consistent interval, while employing a head-adaptive global budget that more aggressively compresses heads with concentrated attention. Backed by a fused IO-aware Triton kernel for block-sparse attention, Step-dLLM applies to pre-trained LLaDA-1.5 and Dream-7B-Instruct without retraining, achieving a 2.78x end-to-end speedup over dense FlashAttention on LLaDA-1.5 at 8K context with 256 denoising steps and 90% sparsity, while improving the accuracy-latency trade-off over prior sparse-attention methods.

Title: Same Function, Different Mechanism: Implementation-Local Geometry Beyond Accuracy

  • Authors: Miroslav Lžičař (Mereology Labs)
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01328
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01328
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01328]] Same Function, Different Mechanism: Implementation-Local Geometry Beyond Accuracy(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01328)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Two models can make exactly the same predictions yet respond differently when their implementations are perturbed. A lookup table can change one case independently; a shared rule couples changes across cases. We compare these mechanisms through an implementation chart: a specified local family of logit perturbations around a fixed predictive distribution. Our main result counts the independent prediction changes allowed by exact finite-group sharing: the active dimension is the number of input-output orbits minus the number of input orbits. For softened modular addition, lookup and shared cyclic charts therefore have ranks $p^2(p-1)$ and $p-1$ despite identical predictions. The classical Fisher pullback connects these dimensions to local KL curvature; mechanism-preserving reparameterizations leave the rank unchanged. We also bound the spectral tail under approximate sharing and separate quadratic rank from higher-order learning coefficients. Trained modular-addition models verify that chart rank is measurable, while controlled compiled transformers distinguish compiler-supported intermediate charts from matched alternatives. Negative controls show where this discrimination fails. The framework evaluates specified mechanism hypotheses; it does not infer a unique mechanism from predictions or predict training dynamics.

Title: The Verifier Is Part of the Simulator: Evidence-View Gaming in Agentic Equation Discovery

  • Authors: Orestis Oikonomou, Bogdan Raonic
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01334
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01334
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01334]] The Verifier Is Part of the Simulator: Evidence-View Gaming in Agentic Equation Discovery(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01334)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: A growing number of agentic pipelines search for governing equations: an agent proposes laws and a verifier checks them against simulated trajectories. The verifier sees only a numerical summary: integrals against test functions on a time grid. That summary carries its own discretization error, making the verifier part of the simulator. We freeze the trajectory, candidate library, verifier and thresholds, leaving the agent one move: which permitted summary to admit. Without scores or language models, finer time grids reduce numerical discrepancies and false library rejections. On PDEBench, the verifier instead accepts equations missing diffusion on score-selected summaries. Maximizing a public score rewarding decisive verdicts and larger mismatches when rejecting the library wrongly rejects all 1,500 in-library cases; retrospectively retuning score constants on a separate bank reduces errors to 13/180 at best. We call this evidence-view gaming: reward hacking by choice of permitted summary. In single-turn forced-choice tests, Claude models rarely choose summaries giving wrong verdicts when asked to be accurate, but often do so when asked to maximize the score or report missing physics. In separate trust prompts, they often rank those harmful summaries least trustworthy. Requiring several summaries to agree reduces errors but often withholds a verdict and performs worse than a fixed fine view on PDEBench: a safeguard, not a certificate. Evidence chosen after its verdict is known cannot alone authorize a claim.

Title: J-Anchor: Defending Against Prefill Attacks via J-Space Selective Refusal Restoration

  • Authors: Running Yang, Beidi Zhao, Xiaoxiao Li
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01342
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01342
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01342]] J-Anchor: Defending Against Prefill Attacks via J-Space Selective Refusal Restoration(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01342)
  • Keywords: defense, attack, robust
  • Abstract: Prefill jailbreaks can cause safety-aligned language models to continue an unsafe assistant prefix even when they would refuse the same request under normal decoding. We study how the model's internal harmfulness and refusal signals evolve along this attacker-controlled trajectory. Using the Jacobian lens, we track vocabulary-grounded harmfulness and refusal signals from the assistant header through prefilling and subsequent generation. We identify a characteristic phenomenon, which we call refusal-state erosion: both the refusal signal and the harmfulness signal weaken as the prefill progresses, but the refusal signal progressively declines to the benign baseline by the end of the prefill, while the harmfulness signal remains above its benign baseline and detectable during subsequent generation. Motivated by this observation, we introduce J-Anchor, a training-free inference-time defense that uses the model's pre-attack harmfulness and refusal signals as a request-specific anchor and selectively restores weakened refusal in J-space during prefilling and decoding. J-Anchor substantially reduces attack success under both fixed and deep prefill attacks across different model families. J-Anchor largely preserves benign behavior and reasoning ability, and ablations highlight the importance of targeted restoration in the J-space refusal subspace. Our results suggest that preserving latent refusal dynamics offers a promising route to robust defense against prefill jailbreaks.

  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Sparse autoencoder (SAE)-based activation steering is a lightweight approach for modifying the behavior of large language models (LLMs) by adding small vectors to hidden states during inference. However, designing effective steering interventions remains challenging. In this work, we develop an information-theoretic framework for analyzing SAE activations through empirical feature-firing distributions. Title: Sensitivity Inflation from Cross-Record Precision Routing in Private Quantized Fine-Tuning

  • Authors: Annan Zhang, Shengjie Song, Wenshuai Yao, Lihao Zhang, Tianmiao
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01411
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01411
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01411]] Sensitivity Inflation from Cross-Record Precision Routing in Private Quantized Fine-Tuning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01411)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Private fine-tuning of large language models increasingly pairs DP-SGD with low-rank adapters on frozen, quantized backbones, and every such stack inherits one unstated premise: frozen layers keep each record's clipped gradient a function of that record alone. We show that LLM.int8 breaks this premise. It routes outlier activation columns to a higher-precision path using a union over the whole batch, so one record decides how every other record's activations are computed, even though the weights are frozen and the quantization scales are per row. We bound the resulting sensitivity inflation, state a record-isolation contract that restores the standard bound, and define record-owned precision dispatch that meets it with the same GEMMs as batched LLM.int8, so isolation needs no extra arithmetic. We then audit the native CUDA stack (bitsandbytes, PEFT, Opacus) against four matched controls. A constructed LoRA-Opacus witness reaches $15.0\times$ the add/remove bound. On pretrained OPT-125M with natural text, other records alter 20% of record-layer precision splits at batch size 16. An exploratory re-clipping analysis finds 10 of 64 candidate neighbors above the bound once every gradient is clipped; the top pair, revalidated natively, reaches $1.065C$ while every control stays within $C$, and forcing one layer's outlier mask removes the excess. A two-million-draw audit of the released adapters yields $\varepsilon$ lower bounds ($3.25$ at $\delta=10^{-5}$) below the advertised guarantee ($4.38$). Resolving an excess this small at the release level would take about 200 times more draws, whereas checking the sensitivity contract directly exposes it in a few native runs, which makes contract checking the practical audit for private quantized training.

Title: TopoSteer: Topological Layer Selection for Activation Steering in LLMs

  • Authors: Tuc Nguyen, Marcos Gutiérrez-del-Pozo, Eduardo Paluzo-Hidalgo, Yifan Hu, Adaku Uchendu, Thai Le
  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01493
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.01493
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.01493]] TopoSteer: Topological Layer Selection for Activation Steering in LLMs(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01493)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Activation steering controls large language models at inference time by adding a vector to their internal representations. Most work asks how to build that vector, and far less attention has gone to where to add it, even though steering at a poorly chosen layer can be worse than not steering at all. Prior selectors choose a layer without ever steering the model to test it: they score how separable the target behavior is at that layer, and how consistently the individual examples point along the steering direction. We argue that what matters is not the separability of the two activation clouds, nor the alignment of individual displacements with their mean, but the extent of the cloud of per-example displacements that mean-difference steering averages. We propose a topology-based layer selection method, called TopoSteer, which scores a layer by the total H_0 persistence of its displacement cloud, computed as the length of the cloud's Euclidean minimum spanning tree. We show that the score focuses on the displacement cloud's relative geometry, while class separation affects it only through standardization. We also provide a theoretical explanation for our method by proving that, as example-level variation increases, TopoSteer and discriminability move in opposite directions, so the two criteria can rank layers in opposite ways. Across multiple language models and steering tasks, TopoSteer provides a principled alternative to discriminability- and separability-based layer selection and yields more effective steering without requiring held-out validation.

To address this limitation, we propose FedUDG. At the federation level, FedUDG utilizes a parameter-functional differentiation strategy that maintains a semantic anchor updated solely using ground-truth labels. Together, these objectives guide the model toward a transferable feature space, while mitigating overfitting to domain-specific characteristics. Furthermore, to address the lack of systematic evaluation of FDG under formal privacy constraints, we develop a sample-level differentially private extension, DP-FedUDG, to quantify the trade-off between privacy and OOD generalization. Expanding on the mechanistic intervention, we propose a test-time adaptive attention head routing to further reduce the impact on performance at matched robustness, pushing beyond the Pareto frontier of current robustness-perception tradeoff.

In this paper, we introduce AR-RRG, an anatomy-guided retrieval-driven method for the generation of radiology reports. AR-RRG consists of two core modules: the anatomy-aware self-training lesion detection network (ASLDNet) and the retrieval-driven radiology report generation (RRRG). ASLDNet leverages anatomy-aware spatial verification (ASV) and class-adaptive dynamic thresholding (CDT) via self-training, delivering accurate lesion localization to support subsequent generation. As the generative branch of AR-RRG, RRRG eliminates clinical hallucinations through dual-granularity retrieval (DR) and complements missing lesions with a neighborhood consensus (NC) mechanism. A parallel continuous visual perception (PCVP) stream converts radiographs into visual tokens through fixed medical encoding and resampling. Multi-scale text and visual features are fused, followed by LoRA-efficient large language model tuning to generate clinically consistent, visually grounded radiology reports. Extensive evaluations on MS-CXR, MIMIC-CXR, and IU X-Ray demonstrate that AR-RRG consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods across mainstream metrics. Our code will be made publicly available.

Title: CUFormer: Modality-Aware CT-Ultrasound Point Cloud Registration for Orthopedic Navigation

  • Authors: Lixing Tan, Qing Xia, Yuting Guo, Shuai Li, Aimin Hao

  • Abstract: Registering intraoperative Ultrasound (US) bone-surface point clouds to preoperative Computed Tomography (CT) models can support radiation-free orthopedic navigation.
