  • Abstract: Federated Learning (FL) offers a paradigm for collaborative machine learning model training among clients, preserving data privacy by avoiding direct data sharing. However, the gradient information exchanged in FL has been shown to be vulnerable to Gradient Inversion Attacks (GIA), which can reconstruct sensitive user data. To counter these threats, various privacy-enhancing technologies like Secure Multi-party Computing (SMC), Homomorphic Encryption (HE), and Differential Privacy (DP) have been integrated into FL. While effective, these methods often introduce significant trade-offs between privacy and model utility. By revisiting the key to privacy exposure in FL under GIA, which lies in the frequent sharing of model gradients that contain private data, we take a new perspective by designing a novel privacy-preserving FL framework that effectively ``breaks the direct connection'' between the shared parameters and the local private data to defend against GIA. Our proposed framework, HyperFL++, leverages hypernetworks to generate local model parameters, where only the hypernetwork parameters are aggregated by the server. To ensure practicality, HyperFL++ incorporates a Row-wise Kronecker Decomposition (RKD) to mitigate the memory and communication overhead from the hypernetwork. Comprehensive theoretical analysis and extensive experiments validate our method's effectiveness in protecting data privacy while maintaining high model utility. Our code is available at this https URL.

Title: Behavior-Discriminative Reward Shaping for Reward-Robust Reinforcement Learning

  • Authors: Zixuan Liu, Fangzheng Wu, Brian Summa, Zizhan Zheng
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02415
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02415
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02415]] Behavior-Discriminative Reward Shaping for Reward-Robust Reinforcement Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02415)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Reward-robust RL typically models misspecification by specifying an uncertainty set that constrains the discrepancy of plausible rewards from a base reward. However, such reward space discrepancies can be behaviorally irrelevant: under potential-based reward shaping (PBRS), many distinct rewards preserve policy ordering. This can introduce substantial redundancy into standard uncertainty sets and degrade optimization performance. We propose Shaping-Aware Reward-Robust RL, which constructs uncertainty sets over PBRS equivalence classes by projecting each reward to a canonical representative, ensuring that the resulting set contains only rewards that induce behaviorally distinct policy rankings. We prove that this projection preserves the optimal robust value while shrinking the uncertainty set and improving empirical performance. Using the connection between robustness and regularization, we obtain a practical algorithm to solve the shaping-aware reward-robust RL problem and enjoy convergence guarantees under standard assumptions. Experiments on benchmarks spanning diverse task domains and levels of complexity show consistent improvements over representative robust RL baselines and exhibit improved robustness to reward perturbations.

Title: A Parameter-Free Best-of-Both-Worlds Policy for Queueing Bandits

  • Authors: Jongyeong Lee, Chansoo Kim
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02431
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02431
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02431]] A Parameter-Free Best-of-Both-Worlds Policy for Queueing Bandits(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02431)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: We study online channel scheduling for a single queue served by $K$ channels under bandit feedback. We propose a simple best-of-both-worlds policy that explores when the queue is empty. During busy periods, it selects the empirical best channel while the prescribed conditions hold and, if any condition is violated, switches to a robust bandit policy to clear the queue. For arbitrary arrivals and services determined by an oblivious adversary, our policy achieves $\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sqrt{K L_T})$ worst-case queue length regret with high probability, where $L_T$ is the longest busy-period length. This result improves the previous $T^{3/4}$ dependence and matches the worst-case lower bound up to logarithmic factors. Under bounded i.i.d. stochastic models with a unique best stabilizing channel, we show that the cumulative queue length regret converges to a finite problem-dependent constant, and further establish the logarithmic expected worst-case queue length regret bound.

Title: M3Former: Fine-Grained Cross-Layer Mixing for Transformers

  • Authors: Jiasong Han, Yunkai Chai, Tong Zhu, Hengyu Zhang, Wei Fang, Jintao Wang, Guoqi Li, Yu Cheng
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02436
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02436
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02436]] M3Former: Fine-Grained Cross-Layer Mixing for Transformers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02436)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: Residual connections give each Transformer block access to all earlier layers only through one fixed sum, broadcast to all of the block's inputs. This broadcast bottleneck imposes three constraints: the query, key, value and residual inputs of a block, which we call its roles, read the same vector; all hidden dimensions within a role share one set of weights over earlier layers; and every earlier layer has the same fixed weight for every token. We propose the Multi-Role Multi-Group Mixing Residual (M3), which removes all three: every role, and every group of hidden dimensions within a role, reads its own token-dependent mixture of all earlier layers. M3 starts as an exact residual connection and adds as little as 1.4% parameters and 1.8% FLOPs. From 100M to 1.4B parameters, M3Former outperforms the Transformer and coarse-grained cross-layer connections at every size, matching a Transformer 1.19-1.34x the size of the one it modifies, trained with 1.4-1.8x the compute. Relaxing only some of the constraints recovers only part of this gain. M3Former also improves sparse mixture-of-experts models of up to 4.6B parameters and trains more stably in deeper networks. Code: this https URL

Title: Evaluating Synthetic Skin Lesion Images for Classifier Training: A Safety-Aware Algorithm

  • Authors: Amindu Kumarasinghe (Southern Cross University), Vinh Bui (Southern Cross University), Reza Ghanbarzadeh (Southern Cross University)
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02437
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02437
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02437]] Evaluating Synthetic Skin Lesion Images for Classifier Training: A Safety-Aware Algorithm(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02437)
  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Skin cancer classifiers need large sets of annotated images, which are scarce, especially for rare lesion types. AI-generated images can fill the gap, but a generator produces images of widely varying quality, so selecting which images to retain is important. That choice usually rests on a single quality score, most often the Fréchet Inception Distance (FID), which has not been tested against what a classifier needs and cannot tell whether the generator memorizes its training photographs. We test that practice with a tournament of 16 quality metrics. Each metric alone selects a training subset from a pool of 275,472 images generated by a diffusion model (EDM2), and a classifier trained on each subset scores the metric per class. The tournament shows that no single metric serves every class: FID computed on Inception features gives the best classifier for one lesion type and no measurable advantage over random sampling for another, and the generation setting it prefers is also the one closest to the training images. We turn these per-class measurements into a safety-aware evaluation algorithm that weights each metric by its measured value in each class and removes images too close to a real training image. On the held-out ISIC-2018 test set, the algorithm improves Macro-$F_2$, a recall-weighted measure of classification performance, over the FID baseline for a Swin-B classifier by +0.0136 to +0.0164 across four safety thresholds (Bonferroni-corrected $p < 0.02$) and over random sampling by about +0.03, though not for ResNet-50. Tightening the threshold improves a memorization-safety score with no measurable change in utility, but every curated set remains less safe than random sampling. The results show a modest but statistically significant gain on the primary classifier, and offer a practical, safety-aware procedure for evaluating and using synthetic data.

Title: Same Sentence, Different Construct: Identity-Swapped Rationales Reveal Measurement Non-Invariance in LLM Hate Speech Classifiers

  • Authors: Wenchang Wang, Yue Song
  • Subjects: cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02491
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02491
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02491]] Same Sentence, Different Construct: Identity-Swapped Rationales Reveal Measurement Non-Invariance in LLM Hate Speech Classifiers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02491)
  • Keywords: protect, large language model
  • Abstract: Large language models are increasingly used as measurement instruments for socially consequential constructs such as hate speech, and are typically validated by their aggregate agreement with human labels. A valid instrument must also apply the same construct to every group it measures. We test this requirement using 421 \hc{} templates that are instantiated identically for seven protected groups, so that only the identity term varies. Four LLM classifiers each exceed 0.95 balanced accuracy, yet within identical templates two of them miss hateful statements about women at markedly higher rates than the same statements about other groups (excess false-negative rates of 8.3 and 4.4 percentage points), a gap first reported for fine-tuned classifiers in 2021. We show how the construct shifts. Comparing rationales within each template, we find that models invoke protected-group status for other groups but recast the identical statement about women as personal dislike or as aimed at an individual, in 22 of 30 missed templates for one model. Adding the benchmark's own definition, which lists sex as a protected characteristic, does not close the gap, and the contrast persists. Apparent false-positive disparities, by contrast, largely vanish under identity control and trace to reclaimed slurs present only for some groups. Errors shared by all models concentrate where model and benchmark definitions diverge, and pairwise counterfactual accuracy adds little beyond endpoint accuracy. We argue that validating LLM measurement requires identity-controlled tests of measurement invariance that examine both decisions and the criteria models state for them.

Title: Semantic Caches for LLM Web Services Leave Their Error Budget Unspent

  • Authors: Hanshuai Cui, Qianli Ma, Zhiqing Tang
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02498
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02498
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02498]] Semantic Caches for LLM Web Services Leave Their Error Budget Unspent(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02498)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Web services built on large language models (LLMs) receive many paraphrases of earlier queries, which a semantic cache answers from stored responses. A reused answer can be wrong, so wrong answers must stay below a share $\delta$ of the queries. Existing caches reuse above a fixed similarity threshold or, like vCache, learn a per-entry rule that bounds each query's error by $\delta$. However, fixed thresholds break the budget on new streams, and per-entry rules need labels on every entry before reusing it. We find an unspent error budget, as vCache realizes only 12-55% of $\delta$ on three public query streams. Surplus spends this budget where reuse is most likely correct. It ranks queries by a risk score shared across paraphrase clusters and opens risk bins as far as an audited error estimate allows. Free labels from every LLM call and a ledger of realized errors keep this threshold safe under drift. On five streams, including ORCAS search queries, Surplus holds $\delta$ in every test cell, which no baseline does. On the shuffled streams it serves 40% more hits than vCache, and its pooled hit rate beats the best baseline holding $\delta$ in each cell by 4.10 points. In a vLLM testbed it cuts the mean latency by 31.59% at the highest load, against 23.67% for vCache.

Title: Ranked but Not Stopped: Detecting Prompt-Injection Hijacks from Typed Decision Models' Output Probabilities

  • Subjects: cs.CL, cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02522
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02522
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02522]] Ranked but Not Stopped: Detecting Prompt-Injection Hijacks from Typed Decision Models' Output Probabilities(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02522)
  • Keywords: attack
  • Abstract: Programs that use a language model as a classifier act on its most probable label. Text injected into the input can move that label to one the attacker chooses, a hijack. We test this monitor on AG News topic classification with five systems, three of them served through a provider's API. Confidence monitors should be tested at their operating alarm rate, against attackers who claim certainty.

  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02551

  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Prompt engineering is commonly used to encourage text-to-image models to generate more detailed images. However, it does not provide reliable, continuous, or disentangled control. We analyze feature tokens produced with and without detail-enhancing prompts across six diffusion transformers and observe a common pattern: these prompts tend to increase the entropy of each token's energy distribution while largely preserving its ordering. That is, flattening the distribution increases the level of detail. Motivated by this counter-intuitive finding, we introduce a simple training-free approach that continuously adjusts the level of detail according to a scalar. Specifically, it shifts the squared channel magnitudes of each token toward a higher entropy distribution with a time-aware scheduler to concentrate the intervention at intermediate sampling steps. Across extensive experiments on different types of diffusion transformers, we show that our approach provides continuous control over detail metrics while largely preserving image quality and semantic structure. For architecture, we propose Omni3DiT, a multi-condition 3D diffusion transformer that distinguishes regions to create from those to retain and fuses image, text, and point guidance for fine-grained control. Experiments across three benchmarks show SeMo3D surpasses state-of-the-art methods on all tasks, delivering multimodal controllability in generation and strong consistency and accuracy in editing and completion. SeMo3D further serves as a unified modeling tool for agents, enabling efficient 3D creation through natural-language interaction. Project page: this https URL.

  • Abstract: Indirect prompt injection creates a distinctive risk for blue-team Large Language Model (LLM) agents in Security Operations Centers (SOCs). MCP architecture favors ease of integration over security, blending the data and control planes into a single natural language channel. This introduces confused deputy and prompt injection vulnerabilities that cannot be completely mitigated in the software development lifecycle (SDLC) of an MCP server. Structured security guidance for server developers has been outpaced by this adoption trend, leaving them without a clear way to identify where vulnerabilities can be addressed at the server layer and where architectural constraints create hard limits. This paper presents a framework based on OWASP MCP Top 10 mapped to three architectural tiers, developed using Design Science Research (DSR). Vulnerabilities are classified as server-primary, gateway-primary, or protocol-primary applying a cascading decision tree following four questions, resulting in reproducible tier assignments and confidence levels independent of who applies it. The framework was validated against an enterprise-grade Managed File Transfer (MFT) MCP server built in Go. Validation results showed that high-confidence server-tier controls worked as predicted, and low-confidence protocol-tier controls failed for architectural reasons beyond server implementation. The architectural ceiling encountered during practical validation is the main contribution and shows how secure-by-design development can reduce the MCP attack surface but not completely mitigate it.

Title: Adaptive Localization of Knowledge Negation for Continual LLM Unlearning

  • Authors: Abudukelimu Wuerkaixi, Qizhou Wang, Sen Cui, Wutong Xu, Bo Han, Gang Niu, Masashi Sugiyama, Changshui Zhang

  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02620
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02620]] Adaptive Localization of Knowledge Negation for Continual LLM Unlearning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02620)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: With the growing deployment of large language models (LLMs) across diverse domains, concerns regarding their safety have grown substantially. LLM unlearning has emerged as a pivotal approach to removing harmful or unlawful content while maintaining utility. Despite increasing interest, the challenges of continual unlearning, which is common in real-world scenarios, remain underexplored. Successive unlearning tasks often lead to intensified utility degradation. To effectively unlearn targeted knowledge while preserving LLM utility, it is essential to minimize changes in model parameters by selectively updating those linked to the target knowledge, thereby ensuring other knowledge remains unaffected. Building on the task vector framework, we propose a new method named ALKN (Adaptive Localization of Knowledge Negation), which uses dynamic masking to sparsify training gradients and adaptively adjusts unlearning intensity based on inter-task relationships. Title: Latent Diffusion-GAN: Adversarial Learning in the Autoencoded Latent Space

  • Authors: U-Chae Jun, Jaeeun Ko, Jiwoo Kang
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02625
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02625
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02625]] Latent Diffusion-GAN: Adversarial Learning in the Autoencoded Latent Space(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02625)
  • Keywords: diffusion, generative
  • Abstract: Diffusion models are powerful generative frameworks for producing high-quality images by denoising latent variables from random noise. However, training with likelihood-based objectives, such as denoising score matching, can lead to locally over-smoothed high-frequency details, including fine textures and sharp edges, thereby limiting perceptual fidelity and structural detail. Adversarial training with GANs enhances sharpness but typically requires additional discriminator networks, increasing computational costs and destabilizing training. To this end, we propose Latent Diffusion Generative Adversarial Networks (LD-GAN), a novel framework that seamlessly integrates adversarial learning into diffusion models without modifying their original pipeline. LD-GAN leverages the pretrained variational autoencoder (VAE) in latent diffusion models as an energy-based discriminator, enabling adversarial training without extra parameters and preserving the structured latent priors learned from large datasets. We also introduce a structural consistency energy that aligns encoder and decoder feature representations, thereby enhancing perceptual quality and compatibility with the pretrained latent space. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LD-GAN significantly improves sample fidelity, perceptual sharpness, and diversity over state-of-the-art baseline methods across various generation tasks while ensuring efficient training dynamics.

  • Abstract: Reconstructing detailed geometry and realistic appearance from a single RGB image is essential yet fundamentally challenging due to inherent ambiguities such as occlusion, lighting variations, and texture-geometry entanglement. Title: Evolving Explanations: Complexity-Adaptive Refinement of Faithful Explanations for Symbolic Regression

  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02634
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02634
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02634]] Evolving Explanations: Complexity-Adaptive Refinement of Faithful Explanations for Symbolic Regression(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02634)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Natural-language explanations generated by large language models (LLMs) for machine learning models often seem fluent but are not necessarily faithful to the underlying model. We formalize this mismatch as a faithfulness gap and propose a complexity-adaptive refinement framework for symbolic regression that iteratively generates and validates explanations until numerical agreement with the target expression is achieved. The approach follows a two-stage pipeline: an explainer LLM produces textual descriptions at increasing levels of detail, while an independent validator LLM extracts a symbolic expression that is compared against the original model.   • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02686
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02686

  • Keywords: segmentation
  • Abstract: Semantic granularity, which refers to the levels at which visual representations organize image content into meaningful entities, is fundamental to image understanding. This paper presents a representation-driven approach for discovering semantically meaningful image regions at different granularities through clustering of pretrained visual representations without semantic supervision. Specifically, we concatenate selected feature maps extracted from a pretrained visual model and apply a singular value decomposition (SVD)-based projection to derive pixel-wise principal representation maps (PRMs). The resulting PRMs capture implicit semantic structures embedded in pretrained visual representations, making them accessible to subsequent semantic clustering. Furthermore, we analyze the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) of SCR profiles to assess representation suitability for discovering semantic regions at multiple granularities. Our results indicate that PRMs exhibiting higher SNR values over an initial range of cluster numbers tend to yield more semantically meaningful regions across multiple granularities. We evaluate the proposed approach on the COCO-2017 and VOC-2012 validation datasets using Hungarian-matched intersection over union (HIoU). Title: ATrust: Adaptive Trust Boundary Expansion for Securing LLM Agents

  • Authors: Xu Zhang, Zhichao Lu
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02741
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02741

  • Keywords: security, privacy, defense, attack, large language model
  • Abstract: As large language model (LLM) agents take on more complex tasks with greater autonomy, security has become a major obstacle to their broader deployment. Unlike in attacks that require complex exploitation of operating system vulnerabilities, attackers can easily hijack agents through indirect prompt injection (IPI), leading to privacy leakage, financial loss, and destructive operations. However, existing defenses often make security decisions based on limited static trusted evidence or assess observations in isolation. Such rigid trust assumptions can cause task-essential information to be unnecessarily discarded, resulting in over-defense and degraded agent functionality. To address this problem, we propose ATrust, a defense mechanism that adaptively expands the trust boundary during agent runtime. Starting from the user query as the root trust anchor, ATrust evaluates external observations against the current trust boundary and execution state, progressively admitting task-essential information as new trust anchors. This preserves necessary environmental feedback while excluding irrelevant content, whether benign or malicious, from subsequent decision-making, thereby limiting unnecessary exposure to external environment. We evaluate ATrust on AgentDojo and AgentDyn, where it achieves the strongest overall security--utility trade-off among existing defenses. With GPT-4o as the engine model, ATrust reduces the attack success rate on AgentDyn from 37.80% to 4.37% while enhancing utility by 10.00%, outperforming AuthGraph, the state-of-the-art defense, by 12.33%. These results demonstrate the effectiveness and practicality of ATrust.

Title: Pair-Relative Partial Equivariance for Rotation-Robust Keypoint Descriptor Matching

  • Authors: Jongmin Lee

  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02751

  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Learned keypoint descriptors trained on upright imagery often fail under large in-plane rotation. Steerers address this by applying a single exact SO(2) representation to the descriptor space, but this global group constraint can be too rigid for keypoint descriptors, whose angular responses need not follow a single group representation. We propose pair-relative partial equivariance, which measures equivariance under the empirical distribution of relative rotations in image pairs, and PaSteer, a relaxed descriptor-steering framework that allows the steering map to leave the exact representation manifold while retaining the lightweight, backbone-free design of steering. PaSteer instantiates this relaxation with two variants: an identity-preserving residual steerer (PaSteer-soft) and a free-form Fourier steerer (PaSteer-free). We show that the per-angle optimal linear steerer for a fixed non-equivariant descriptor generally does not obey the group composition law, explaining why exact representations can be suboptimal in the frozen-descriptor regime. On Roto-360, MegaDepth-1500, AIMS, and IMC-PT, PaSteer consistently improves rotation-robust matching over an exact steerer on frozen DeDoDe descriptors, especially under large relative rotations, with minimal upright degradation.

Title: Unlocking the Potential of Vision Foundation Models for Multi-Task Dense Prediction via Mixture of Task Adapters

  • Authors: Yunzhi Zhuge, Xinzhuo Yu, Sitong Gong, Lu Zhang, Pingping Zhang, Huchuan Lu
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02769
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02769
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02769]] Unlocking the Potential of Vision Foundation Models for Multi-Task Dense Prediction via Mixture of Task Adapters(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02769)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Multi-task dense prediction seeks a single, coherent representation that supports simultaneously discriminative semantic recognition and regularized geometric/structural inference. For vision foundation models, pretrained semantic invariances conflict with the frequency and smoothness biases of dense objectives; joint fine-tuning and single-route adapters therefore compress competing gradients into one update channel, causing negative transfer and unstable cross-task agreement. We frame this as two-level adaptation: task-generic alignment that preserves priors, and task-aware specialization that resolves late-stage conflicts. D2MoE introduces Task-Generic Low-Rank Adaptation (TG-LoRA) in attention projections throughout the network to provide a stable, shared adaptation channel, and a Task-Adaptive Mixture of Experts (TA-MoE) injected only at the final attention block of each stage to enact sparse, task-conditioned perturbations of token mixing in a single forward pass. A Semantic Prior Injector further couples multi-level task features with frozen foundation priors to reinforce semantic coherence while preserving boundary and structural fidelity. Experiments across diverse benchmarks substantiate that this structured factorization yields robust multi-task improvements with a small trainable footprint, establishing a principled and scalable route to prior-preserving, foundation-centric multi-task dense perception.

Title: Spectral Degeneration of Self-Attention under Isotropic Score Geometry

  • Authors: Mengda Li, Jianfeng Yao
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, stat.ML
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02778
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02778
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02778]] Spectral Degeneration of Self-Attention under Isotropic Score Geometry(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02778)
  • Keywords: transformer
  • Abstract: We study the spectral structure of the self-attention matrix in Transformer models through an idealized spectral model for the pre-softmax score matrix. Motivated by the rotational isotropy of query and key representations, our model replaces the random token-space orientation of the score matrix by its canonical singular-value representative with a logarithmic spike--bulk separation. Empirical diagnostics on pretrained vision and language transformers motivate this model: token-space singular matrices have nearly uniform eigen-angle distributions, and score spectra are dominated by a small number of effective directions. We then prove that the row-wise softmax map transforms this structure into a degenerate attention matrix: dominant modes are preserved, whereas the bulk spectral distributions concentrate at zero. Stable rank remains bounded for bidirectional attention but grows logarithmically for causal attention. Within the canonical model, these results explain how attention can preserve a small number of dominant directions while compressing the remaining bulk modes.

Title: Lookahead Prediction-Guided Adaptive Visual Refocusing for Interleaved Multimodal Reasoning

  • Authors: Mengzhao Wang, Yanli Ji, Peng Ye
  • Subjects: cs.CV
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02780
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02780
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02780]] Lookahead Prediction-Guided Adaptive Visual Refocusing for Interleaved Multimodal Reasoning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02780)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Interleaved multimodal chain-of-thought reasoning improves the visual reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models by dynamically incorporating visual evidence during reasoning. However, existing methods primarily select evidence from historical context. When the reasoning focus shifts, history-guided evidence may remain focused on prior regions and fail to cover the visual information required by the next step. We refer to this lag between visual evidence and subsequent reasoning demands as Temporal Evidence Myopia. To address this issue, we propose Lookahead Prediction-guided Adaptive Visual Refocusing (LPAVR), a training-free test-time framework. LPAVR transforms Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) from a decoding acceleration mechanism into a lookahead signal for anticipating upcoming reasoning intent. During MTP decoding, the draft head generates multiple future tokens, while the target model verifies them in parallel and retains the longest prefix consistent with its decoding trajectory. This target-verified prefix contains near-future semantics, which LPAVR uses to construct lookahead-guided visual evidence and assess whether history-guided evidence covers the indicated regions. If the coverage is sufficient, the history-guided evidence is retained; otherwise, LPAVR switches to the lookahead-guided evidence. This better aligns the visual context with upcoming reasoning and improves consistency between draft predictions and target-model decoding. Experiments on multiple multimodal reasoning benchmarks show that LPAVR improves both reasoning accuracy and the acceptance rates of MTP speculative tokens, jointly enhancing visual reasoning and speculative decoding efficiency.

  • Abstract: Autoregressive video diffusion models (AR-VDMs) enable real-time, long-horizon video generation but degrade rapidly once the inference horizon outruns the training horizon, due to compounding exposure bias. Title: Iterated Narrow Networks: Turing Completeness at Width 4 on Convex Input Spaces and the Complexity of Verification

  • Authors: Olivier Bournez, Johanne Cohen, Adrian Wurm
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.CC, cs.LO, cs.NE
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02840
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02840
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02840]] Iterated Narrow Networks: Turing Completeness at Width 4 on Convex Input Spaces and the Complexity of Verification(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02840)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: We study the iterates of a fixed ReLU network $N$ of width 4, that is, the discrete-time system $x_{k+1} = N(x_k)$ on $[0,1]^4$. Equivalently, $x_k$ is the output of a weight-tied network made of $k$ copies of $N$, or of a recurrent network with no external input, started at $x_0$. We prove that every deterministic Turing machine $M$ admits such a network $N_M$. The network can be constructed in polynomial time from $M$. Each iteration has depth $\mathrm{poly}(|M|)$. The iterates of $N_M$ simulate $M$ exactly on every encoded tape. The number of iterations is proportional to the running time of $M$. Moreover, the fourth coordinate irreversibly flags every point of the convex cube $[0,1]^4$ whose simulation reads an invalid digit. This flag is the certificate that makes hardness reductions sound on convex input regions, the regime of practical verifiers. Convex regions were previously inaccessible because of a continuity obstruction inherent to ReLU networks. The correspondence has three consequences. First, reachability for iterated width-4 ReLU networks spans the full complexity hierarchy along the single axis of the time bound: it is NP-complete for a unary bound, NEXP-complete for a binary bound and RE-complete (undecidable) with no bound. Second, two extra neurons of width suffice to extend the hardness to every common smooth activation: at width 6, the robust verification problem (a promise problem with $\delta/2\delta$ gap) is NP-complete for $\tanh$, sigmoid, GELU, Swish, ELU, and any polynomial-time-computable continuous non-polynomial activation with a polynomial-time-computable Lipschitz constant. Third, the whole spectrum transfers to discretized Neural ODEs in dimension 5.

Title: Gibbs Variational Last Layers for Long-Horizon Dynamics Learning

  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.RO
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02891
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02891

  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Accurate long-horizon probabilistic predictions of dynamical systems are a prerequisite for model-based decision-making. Variational Bayesian last layers (VBLLs) are an attractive model class for this setting, offering tractable uncertainty at near-deterministic cost, yet they are trained exclusively with single-step likelihood objectives that may be insufficient for reliable multi-step rollouts. We revisit VBLLs through the lens of Gibbs variational inference, which decouples posterior inference from the choice of training loss. Title: Unlocking the Potential of Contrastive Regularization for Diffusion Transformer Training

  • Authors: Yi Tan, Beier Zhu, Sirui Chen, Yunzhe Lin, Yuan Zhou, Cheng Chen, Xingyu Zhu, Tianrun Chen, Yanbin Hao, Hanwang Zhang
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02921
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02921
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02921]] Unlocking the Potential of Contrastive Regularization for Diffusion Transformer Training(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02921)
  • Keywords: diffusion, transformer
  • Abstract: Representation regularization has emerged as an effective way to improve diffusion transformer training. Yet one of the most canonical regularizers--InfoNCE-style contrastive learning--remains largely underexplored in this setting. We naturally ask a question: can standard contrastive learning serve as an effective regularizer for diffusion transformers? Surprisingly, we find that naive InfoNCE regularization brings only marginal gains. Our analysis reveals that the objective is easily dominated by a shortcut: near-orthogonal noise components in diffusion inputs provide a bypass for enlarging negative-pair distances while weakening positive-pair alignment, especially at high noise levels. Specifically, CRAN anchors contrastive dispersion with clean-sample representation distances, transferring clean relational semantic structure to noisy diffusion representations to suppress the noise shortcut. Extensive experiments on ImageNet show that CRAN consistently improves diffusion transformer training across model scales and training budgets, outperforming internal feature-alignment and dispersive regularization baselines while matching external DINO-guided REPA. These results re-establish InfoNCE-based contrastive learning as an effective regularizer for diffusion training, once its noise-driven over-dispersion is properly constrained.

Title: Balancing Representations by Identifying and Generating Under-represented Data

  • Authors: Filipe Laitenberger, Hirokatsu Kataoka, Yuki Asano, Cees Snoek, Mohammadreza Salehi
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02952
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02952
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02952]] Balancing Representations by Identifying and Generating Under-represented Data(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02952)
  • Keywords: diffusion, segmentation
  • Abstract: Self-supervised learning inherits the coverage of its source data. When an unlabeled corpus is long-tailed, the representation becomes dense around frequent modes and thin where coverage is weak. Objectives for long-tailed SSL reweight the images already in the corpus and add no new ones. We introduce BRIDGE (Balancing Representations by Identifying and Generating Under-represented Data), a label-free feedback loop around the source set. The current encoder scores every source image by its mean kNN distance, and a budgeted acquisition rule selects images from a band of that score distribution where support is thin and the generator still produces faithful variants. A frozen image-to-image diffusion model then adds local variants of the selected anchors, and pretraining continues on the enlarged source set. We formulate acquisition as recoverability-weighted allocation of local support and derive when a finite budget should favor a sparse band that excludes the extreme tail and when reallocation after each repair is optimal under a fixed objective. Across five long-tailed, web-scraped and synthetic source regimes, BRIDGE has the highest seven-task linear-probe and kNN average of the compared methods, and paired experiments extend the gain to ViT-S under contrastive and self-distillation objectives, to low-label fine-tuning and to semantic segmentation. The linear-probe gain is smaller under masked reconstruction. Generated repair matches an oracle that restores the withheld real images, and under a frozen encoder the inserted images shrink the neighborhoods of the selected anchors about twice as much as those of matched unselected images. BRIDGE needs no labels and leaves the pretraining objective unchanged, so it can be added to pretraining on uncurated web data, where the encoder decides which images to generate.

Title: Cyber-JEV: Matched Contrastive Fine-Tuning of Language Models for Fast Source-Code Vulnerability Triage

  • Authors: Zhuyi Zhao, Shengjie Yuan, Shengyu Wang, Kang Wei, Wang Yang, Wenchao Xu, Fushuo Huo
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02957
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.02957
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.02957]] Cyber-JEV: Matched Contrastive Fine-Tuning of Language Models for Fast Source-Code Vulnerability Triage(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02957)
  • Keywords: security
  • Abstract: Single-pass scoring models such as Jev give each code function a vulnerability probability, so that security teams can rank many functions for review without generating written explanations. Training an open-weight model for this task is limited by data. Benchmarks with both vulnerable and non-vulnerable labels keep few vulnerable functions once examples related to the test data are removed, while larger public vulnerability records mostly contain vulnerable functions only. Mixing the two lets the data source predict the label, and training on the labeled data alone leaves a model that often scores a fixed function above its vulnerable original. We present Cyber-JEV, a fine-tuning method that uses fix commits to turn these records into comparison groups. Each group holds a vulnerable function, its patched version and up to two unchanged functions from the same file, so the model sees the change that removed a recorded defect. One shared model learns two tasks: classifying functions from the labeled reference data, and ranking the vulnerable function above the other members of its group. At test time the input is one function and the output is a vulnerability score. On a test benchmark of 7,270 functions, an ensemble of five fine-tuned Qwen3.5-4B models reaches an area under the receiver operating characteristic curve of 0.814, compared with 0.743 for Jev 1.13. With the 4,031 alerts of Jev as the review budget, it misses 16 of 322 vulnerable functions instead of 39. Compared with training on the reference data alone, it ranks the vulnerable version above the patched version 14.8 to 29.2 percentage points more often on three sets of real fixes. Such scores can order the changed functions of a pull request or a release for review under a fixed budget.

  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Generative recommendation has been widely adopted in large-scale industrial recommendation systems, where multimodal item embeddings are quantized into offline semantic identifiers (SIDs) and items are retrieved by autoregressively generating their SIDs. However, conventional SID construction often underrepresents item utility reflected in post-exposure behaviors such as shares, long views, and video completion, making semantically similar items with different utility insufficiently distinguishable while struggling to accommodate rapidly changing utility signals. Moreover, generation likelihood may be misaligned with downstream utility, causing high-utility candidates to be prematurely pruned during beam search before post-generation reranking or training-time reward alignment can effectively compensate. To address these limitations, we propose UAGR, which aligns both SID construction and decoding with downstream multi-objective utility. Our Utility-Enriched Semantic Codes preserve the original semantic information while incorporating item-level utility without increasing SID length or vocabulary size, supporting incremental utility updates with no additional tokenization or sequence-decoding cost. Meanwhile, our Utility-Guided Decoding estimates candidate utility by combining predictions of multiple post-exposure outcomes and incorporates these estimates into beam scoring, reducing the risk of prematurely pruning high-utility paths while enabling flexible utility control without interfering with generative backbone training. Extensive offline experiments and online A/B tests under real-world traffic demonstrate the effectiveness and deployability of UAGR, which has been fully deployed on Kuaishou and increases video watch time by 0.206\% online.

Title: Systematic Derivation of Leakage in Massey Secret Sharing Schemes

  • Authors: Nadja Aoutouf (X, SURYCAT), Daniel Augot (X, SURYCAT)
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03009
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03009
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03009]] Systematic Derivation of Leakage in Massey Secret Sharing Schemes(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03009)
  • Keywords: attack
  • Abstract: Leakage attacks on secret sharing schemes can be based on the exploitation of partial information about individual shares to recover the underlying secret. In coding theory, linear exact repair schemes (LERSs) allow the recovery of a codeword symbol from limited information obtained from the remaining symbols. When applied to code-based secret sharing schemes, such repair schemes can be interpreted as recovering the secret from partial information about the shares, expressed in terms of subfield symbols. Guruswami and Wootters introduced the first explicit constructions of LERSs for low-rate Reed--Solomon codes that is optimal in terms of bandwidth. In this work, we first investigate the construction and algebraic properties of LERSs, building on the Guruswami-Wootters construction. We derive new explicit repair schemes from their construction and establish structural properties of the resulting schemes. Second, we further develop a systematic computational approach based on a Gr{ö}bner bases approach to identify leakage functions associated with LERSs for arbitrary linear codes. This approach enables us to investigate the existence and structural properties of LERSs across different code families and parameter ranges. We first applied our method to Reed-Solomon codes, which gave as further insights into the algebraic structure of LERSs. Moreover, by applying our method to generalized Reed-Muller and Tamo-Barg codes, we identify several LERSs that, to the best of our knowledge, have not previously been described in the literature.

Title: Comparative study of global pooling for pre-departure ship fuel consumption prediction: Advantages over per-ship and adaptation-based approaches

  • Authors: Heungjun Im, Bongmin Cha, Agung Nugraha, Donghyun Kim, Seongphil Kang, Minwook Choi, Jeongmin Park, Jihwan Lee
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03015
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03015
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03015]] Comparative study of global pooling for pre-departure ship fuel consumption prediction: Advantages over per-ship and adaptation-based approaches(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03015)
  • Keywords: federate, fair, transformer
  • Abstract: Most fuel oil consumption (FOC) models for voyage planning are per-ship models and thus generalize poorly beyond each vessel's records. Transfer and federated learning have been reported to perform better, but under different protocols that often use inputs unavailable before departure or random data splits. It therefore remains unclear which approach performs best. In this study, we compare 11 per-ship, adaptation-based, and global methods on 21 vessels. To prevent leakage, all methods receive identical inputs known or forecast before departure, excluding engine RPM and shaft power, and are tested on intact voyage legs after all training records of each vessel. Global pooling, in which one model describes each vessel by its ship identity and seven ship specifications, performs best overall in every setting examined. For in-fleet vessels, Global LightGBM reaches an overall MAPE of 8.92%, against 11.14% for Per-Ship LightGBM, and outperforms every per-ship and adaptation-based model. Global models of five architectures, including a Transformer, all outperform the per-ship and federated models. Fine-tuning a global model for each vessel, even by low-rank adaptation of the Transformer, adds no accuracy. Without any records of a vessel, the global model reaches a median MAPE of 11.20% and outperforms a per-ship model trained on that vessel's records for 14 of 21 vessels. Adding its first records to the pooled data is as accurate as vessel-specific adaptation. Ship identity and specifications are largely interchangeable for in-fleet vessels, but only the specifications support zero-shot prediction. Its error grows as a vessel's specifications differ from the fleet's. Per-ship models and vessel-specific adaptation are therefore unnecessary under fair comparison. One global model can serve a whole fleet, including newly delivered vessels with similar specifications.

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Numerous distillation methods have been developed to accelerate diffusion models. However, current works predominantly rely on a simple combination of objectives, leaving the question of how to effectively synergize them largely underexplored. In this paper, we specifically investigate the synergy between Score Distillation and Adversarial Distillation. Title: Improved regret bounds in tabular RL under local differential privacy

  • Authors: Hugo Richard (ENSAE, FAIRPLAY)
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03026
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03026
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03026]] Improved regret bounds in tabular RL under local differential privacy(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03026)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: We study regret minimization for time-homogeneous episodic tabular reinforcement learning with $S$ states and $A$ actions under non-interactive $\epsilon$-local differential privacy (LDP). In each episode $k \in [K]$, the decision-maker sends a policy to a user, the user executes the policy for $H$ steps, and sends an $\epsilon$-locally differentially private message back to the decision-maker. When $H=1$, the problem reduces to an LDP contextual bandit with $S$ contexts, $A$ arms and horizon $K$. We prove that the minimax regret is $\min\bigg(K, \frac{ \sqrt{\max( S, \exp(\epsilon) + 1) S A K (\exp(\epsilon) + 1)}}{\exp(\epsilon) - 1}\bigg)$, up to constants and a $\sqrt{\log(A)}$ factor. The upper bound is achieved by an adaptation of EXP3 and a unary encoding mechanism. When $H > 1$, and for small $\epsilon$, we show a lower bound in $\Omega(HS\frac{\sqrt{A K}}{\epsilon})$ and an upper bound in $\tilde{O}(H^2 S^2 \frac{\sqrt{ AK}}{\epsilon})$. The upper bound is achieved by an algorithm based on UCBVI and a unary encoding mechanism. For small $\epsilon$, these bounds improve the previously known lower bound by a factor $\sqrt{S}$ and the previously known upper bound by a factor of $H \sqrt{A}$.

We provide actionable guidance based on data properties, error tolerances, and tuning budgets, identify impactful hyperparameters, and characterize algorithm robustness to these choices.

Title: Bayesian Sequential Optimal Experimental Design for Single-Pixel Imaging using Reinforcement Learning

  • Authors: Marcos Antonio Obando, Tianyuan Wang, Tanja Tarvainen, Tristan van Leeuwen, Felix Lucka
  • Subjects: cs.CV, cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03042
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03042
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03042]] Bayesian Sequential Optimal Experimental Design for Single-Pixel Imaging using Reinforcement Learning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03042)
  • Keywords: robust
  • Abstract: Adaptive compressed sensing (CS) aims to select the most informative measurements sequentially, which is crucial for many modern imaging systems. This task can be formulated as a Bayesian sequential optimal experimental design (sOED) problem, where each new acquisition is chosen to maximally reduce uncertainty about the unknown image given prior information and subsequent data provided by acquisitions. However, in single-pixel imaging (SPI), the large design space consists of all possible binary illumination patterns. Directly optimizing the Bayesian sOED objective over this space using conventional methods is generally challenging. In this work, we reformulate sOED for SPI as a reinforcement learning (RL) problem. In RL, an agent learns a policy through repeated experimental trials to maximize a reward, here given as the expected information gain, the most complete measure of uncertainty reduction in Bayesian OED. This avoids the need to directly optimize complex information-theoretic objectives during a measurement to obtain the next design. Instead, the result is a learned policy function that directly maps the current posterior belief about the image to the next optimal binary measurement pattern, in a computationally efficient way. Using a simple yet representative SPI imaging scenario, this work evaluates the scalability of the learned policy under two different policy parameterizations. Furthermore, we investigate how performance deteriorates when only approximate posterior information is available or the measurements are impaired by noise, and propose several strategies to introduce additional design constraints motivated by biomedical applications. The numerical experiments demonstrate that the proposed Bayesian sOED-RL framework remains robust and can be extended to more complex, large-scale SPI scenarios.

Title: Optimal Subgroup Discovery at Every Support Threshold

  • Authors: Lincen Yang, Qi Huang, Niki van Stein, Thomas Bäck, Matthijs van Leeuwen, Zhong Li
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03053
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03053
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03053]] Optimal Subgroup Discovery at Every Support Threshold(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03053)
  • Keywords: generative
  • Abstract: Subgroup discovery aims to identify regions of the feature space where a target variable deviates from its marginal distribution. A fundamental challenge in this problem is the trade-off between a subgroup's deviation and its support: small subgroups can be highly deviating but statistically meaningless, while large ones cannot deviate much by construction. Existing methods tackle this challenge by collapsing the two into a single scalar quality measure, implicitly committing to one particular trade-off and making it difficult to recover the most deviating subgroup under a user-specified support constraint. We instead provide the first theoretical and structural characterization of optimal subgroups with maximum Kullback-Leibler (KL) divergence under arbitrary support constraints. Under a partition-based generative model, where the feature space decomposes into regions of homogeneous conditional distributions of the target variable called atoms, we prove that every point on the deviation-support Pareto front is a union of whole atoms together with at most one fractional atom. This reduces an uncountable search to a finite combinatorial problem. Guided by this theory, we propose a simple two-step recipe: learn a homogeneous partition, then select atoms greedily. Across 19 benchmark datasets, it finds the most deviating subgroups at a range of support thresholds, and reliably improves subgroups returned by existing methods when applied as a post-processing step. These results empirically validate the sufficiency and necessity of our theoretical contributions.

  • Authors: Lakshmi Narayanan P, Narenkarthik K, Naren Kumar C

Conversely, Large Language Models (LLMs) can reason across sentences, but may draw conclusions the text does not support. Contrary to our hypothesis, fusion degrades performance, dropping Macro-F1 to 0.7392, 0.6730, and 0.5991, respectively. To understand this decline, we analyze agreements, disagreements, and common error patterns between the NLI and LLM verifiers. Our error analysis shows that NLI and LLM disagreements often result in correct LLM predictions being overturned.

We evaluate Ray-SCR across multiple SCR architectures and on datasets with pinhole, fisheye, and omnidirectional cameras.   • Keywords: large language model

Knowledge distillation provides a practical way to build compact surgical MLLMs, yet direct distillation is not well suited to surgical data because surgical samples vary greatly in difficulty, visual ambiguity, and required clinical context, making uniform teacher-student transfer unstable. In addition, the role of supervision changes during training: early stages require stronger teacher guidance for multimodal alignment, while later stages rely more on task supervision for effective optimization. To address these challenges, we propose SurgPace, a complexity-aware continuous curriculum framework for progressive distillation of surgical vision-language models. SurgPace estimates sample complexity using predictive confidence and Lorentz geometric consistency, and uses the fused score to guide a continuous easy-to-hard training process. We further introduce hierarchical representation alignment, which aligns the student with the teacher at the feature, logit, and probability levels, while gradually shifting the training focus from teacher imitation to task supervision. This design separates sample-level curriculum from objective-level scheduling, leading to more stable and effective distillation. We evaluate SurgPace on Surg-396K, a benchmark built from four public surgical datasets and covering five dialogue paradigms. SurgPace consistently outperforms strong MLLM distillation baselines, demonstrating its effectiveness for building compact, memory-efficient surgical MLLMs with faster decoding under controlled benchmarks. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: An Information Hierarchy of Privacy Filters and Odometers

  • Authors: Sophie Taylor, Praneeth Vippathalla, Justin P. Coon
  • Subjects: cs.CR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03130
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03130
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03130]] An Information Hierarchy of Privacy Filters and Odometers(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03130)
  • Keywords: privacy
  • Abstract: In sequential data analysis, privacy loss accumulates over time, and privacy filters and odometers are used to control and track this cumulative cost. We study how the information used by differential privacy filters and odometers fundamentally limits what they can achieve. We formalise three levels of information: spec-level information, fixed prior to the interaction; mechanism-level information, which includes the mechanisms instantiated during the interaction; and realisation-level information, which further includes the realised interaction. We classify privacy filters and odometers by the least informative level sufficient to determine their behaviour. Our main results establish strict and arbitrarily large separations in achievable performance across this information hierarchy. For privacy filters, moving up the hierarchy can yield an unbounded utility advantage, quantified by a clipped measure of the number of allowed releases. For privacy odometers, it can yield an arbitrarily large improvement in precision, quantified by the expected peak excess of the reported bound over the realised privacy loss. Further, although the information classes are distinct, we show that moving to a richer class need incur essentially no loss in performance. Finally, we show that randomisation provides a resource orthogonal to information: at every information level, it can yield an arbitrarily large utility improvement over deterministic filters. Thus, information access and randomisation can be viewed as fundamental resources governing privacy procedures in adaptive interactions.

Title: DepthGraft: Structural Regularization through Hierarchical Cross-Layer KV Reconstruction

  • Authors: Xiaohan Qin, Xiangdong Zhang, Yu Wang, Huaijin Wu, Zhuo Xia, Yebin Yang, Junchi Yan

  • Abstract: Scaling depth is a key route to stronger large language models, but Pre-LN architectures often suffer from depth-wise information dilution, limiting the effective use of deep layers. We revisit cross-layer KV sharing as an efficient form of such connectivity. Beyond cache compression, each KV reuse edge creates a direct gradient path from deep layers to source KV projections, turning KV sharing into an implicit structural regularizer for KV representation geometry. These results validate the potential of DepthGraft as a scalable design for large-scale pretraining. The project page is available at this https URL.

Title: FlowBatt: Flow Matching for Probabilistic Battery Degradation Prediction

  • Authors: Hamidreza Eivazi, Eslam Sharaawy, Stefan Wittek, André Hebenbrock, Raphael Ginster, Steffen Blömeke

  • Abstract: Battery degradation remains a central challenge in the development and broad application of sustainable energy technologies. By generating multiple plausible trajectories, FlowBatt provides empirical prediction intervals for battery degradation modeling. These results indicate that flow-matching-based trajectory modeling is a promising framework for probabilistic battery health prediction.

  • Abstract: Spherical transformer configurations can accelerate grokking through simultaneous modifications of several model properties. The present study investigated the contributions of these modifications in the published Sphere+Fourier configuration using 960 controlled modular-arithmetic runs. A complete factorial analysis varied residual projection, readout weight normalization, logit scale, and weight decay. Across four matched settings with weight decay, the successful-run mean crossing times without projection were 8-37 times those observed with projection.   • Abstract: Deploying AI at the edge increases the need for security features on existing devices. When physical access to the device is available, storing secret keys in non-volatile memory is vulnerable. Physical unclonable functions (PUFs) address this by exploiting the unique yet repeatable characteristics of each device, but conventional implementations require design-stage modifications, making them inapplicable to a vast number of existing devices already manufactured. Recently, software PUFs have emerged as an alternative approach, leveraging analog characteristics exposed through software-only interfaces to derive unique responses, without post-fabrication changes. Since edge AI hardware is bound to long operational lifespans, this offers clear economic and engineering value. Existing research on software PUFs remains fragmented. It lacks a holistic review of the trade-offs among choices of components used as entropy sources and software methods to expose analog characteristics. This gap hinders assessing feasibility, comparing approaches, and identifying open challenges towards a hardware root of trust in existing devices. This paper surveys 37 existing software PUF works and compares them against the requirements of on-device model protection: boot-time versus runtime key derivation, and response latency. It analyzes trade-offs, outlines four key challenges that current software PUFs face for edge AI deployment, and highlights promising directions for overcoming barriers to real-world adoption.

Title: Do Agents Still Follow the Rules? Benchmarking Rule Compliance under Agent Context Compaction

  • Authors: Yiwei Zha, Qingyu Meng, Herbert Bos, Min Chen
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03223
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03223
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03223]] Do Agents Still Follow the Rules? Benchmarking Rule Compliance under Agent Context Compaction(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03223)
  • Keywords: defense, attack
  • Abstract: LLM agents compact long histories to fit their context windows, but the compacted context must still carry the operating rules that govern later actions. Prior work shows that violations rise after compaction, but measures compliance outside a complete agent harness and does not identify where each violation comes from. We introduce ARCBench(Agent Rule Compliance Benchmark), a configurable benchmark built on the open-source pi agent harness. Its tool-calling agent loop lets us measure rule compliance from actions taken during execution, bringing the evaluation closer to deployed agent workflows. Using ARCBench, we examine where violations after compaction come from, and how different compaction configurations and adversarial settings change their sources. We find that compaction rarely deletes the rule: most violations after compaction occur while the rule is still in the compacted history, with the agent treating a later request as permission, ignoring the rule, and proceeding anyway. Where violations come from depends on the configuration: how the rule is delivered decides whether the agent would break it even without compaction, while the compaction strategy and budget decide whether the rule survives. The strongest compactor-targeted attack more than doubles violations by corrupting the rule in the summary and changing how the agents behave, whereas the strongest defense restores the rule in most summary but still violates. Finally, retaining a rule does not guarantee compliance: compacted runs that retain the rule violate more than twice as often as runs with the full history.

  • Abstract: Multimodal test-time adaptation (TTA) enables pretrained multimodal models to accommodate distribution shifts during inference using only unlabeled test data, without recourse to the original training data. This complexity is particularly pronounced under modality reliability shifts, which preserve modality availability but degrade information quality, and modality availability shifts, which render the corresponding modality entirely inaccessible. Title: R$^2$-Omni: Reducing Structural and Precision Redundancy in Omni-LLMs for Long-Form Generation

  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Omni-modal large language models (Omni-LLMs) typically process large numbers of multimodal tokens from audio-visual inputs, incurring substantial computational costs and a large memory footprint for the key-value (KV) cache. Aggressive token pruning can alleviate this problem while maintaining competitive performance on multiple-choice tasks, which require only a single option as output. However, we observe substantial quality degradation when generating long-form, open-ended responses. We attribute this gap to query-guided selection, which can retain sufficient evidence for answer selection while discarding details needed for comprehensive generation. This motivates reducing overlapping information across tokens, termed structural redundancy, while preserving diverse content. However, the retained representations may still contain precision redundancy due to unnecessarily high numerical precision in their cached values. To address these two levels of redundancy in token representations, we introduce a training-free framework named R$^2$-Omni. First, Structure-Aware Token Consolidation (STC) consolidates redundant representations using spatiotemporal similarity in video and local continuity in audio. Then, Semantic-Sensitivity Guided Precision Allocation (SSPA) allocates value precision to the retained representations according to the estimated reduction in attention-output error under quantized keys, with query attention and audio-visual alignment providing a semantic prior. Extensive experiments on long-form generation benchmarks demonstrate an average 5.0$\times$ reduction in the memory footprint while preserving 97.98% of baseline generation quality and achieving a 1.48$\times$ end-to-end speedup, highlighting the benefits of jointly reducing structural and precision redundancy.

  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Zeroth-order (ZO) optimization has emerged as a promising approach for memory-efficient large language model (LLM) post-training, yet its reliable application to reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) remains largely unexplored. Motivated by the distinct data regimes of on-policy RL and fixed-data optimization, we develop a generic variance-theoretic framework to investigate what limits the reliability of ZO gradient estimation. Our analysis reveals that estimation reliability is structurally determined by whether the two finite-difference evaluations are performed on matched data. On-policy reinforcement learning (RL) under the mismatched-data regime produces a $1/\mu^2$ oracle-noise amplification that sets an irreducible noise floor as the perturbation scale $\mu$ decreases, whereas Boltzmann Targeted SFT (BOLT) under the matched-data regime eliminates this amplification via noise cancellation. We further derive convergence guarantees with explicit constants that cleanly separate the two regimes. These results naturally lead to ZO-BOLT, which applies ZO optimization to BOLT's RLVR-aligned weighted-SFT objective. Controlled perturbation-scale sweeps and matched-versus-mismatched end-to-end comparisons validate the predicted effect of data matching. Experiments across three model variants and two mathematical reasoning benchmarks further show that ZO-BOLT consistently improves over the pretrained baseline while retaining a near-inference memory footprint. The code is publicly available at this https URL.

  • Abstract: In node selection tasks, the goal is to identify a single unknown target node in a graph. Classical evaluation metrics for such tasks are either statistically ill-conceived or blind to the graph structure. To fill this gap, we introduce two strictly proper scoring rules that are explicitly graph-aware: the Resistance score, which is parameter-free, and the Diffusion score, which operates at an adjustable coarse-graining resolution. The Resistance score generalises two classical scoring rules: the multi-category Brier score and the Ranked probability score. Applied to rooted trees, it also serves as the strictly proper version of the improper Hierarchical error distance used in hierarchical classification. In the same setting, we furthermore establish strict propriety of the Hierarchical cross-entropy family, a recently introduced separate class of hierarchy-aware scoring rules. Experiments on epidemic source detection and on ImageNet classification confirm that the proposed scores detect meaningful differences in estimation quality that classical metrics miss.

However, existing methods still require the anchor image pose to be known at inference, significantly limiting their practical use. The relative pose estimator is trained exclusively on synthetic face renderings, without real pose supervision, pseudo-labels, domain adaptation, or fine-tuning on the evaluation sets. On real 6DoF benchmarks, ReVA-HPE achieves state-of-the-art rotation accuracy on BIWI and, given a near-frontal enrollment image, on FaMoS, while remaining competitive in metric translation despite relying solely on synthetic pose supervision. Title: ENGINE: Endogenous Variational MultiScale Optimization for Zeroth-Order LLM Fine-Tuning

  • Authors: Zhuoli Ouyang, Changxi Chi, Siyuan Li, Tailin Wu
  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03293
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03293
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03293]] ENGINE: Endogenous Variational MultiScale Optimization for Zeroth-Order LLM Fine-Tuning(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03293)
  • Keywords: large language model
  • Abstract: Zeroth-order (ZO) optimization enables memory-efficient large language model (LLM) fine-tuning, and recent methods incorporate curvature-aware strategies. However, in the high-dimensional parameter space of LLMs, these methods inherently suffer from exploding gradient variance and prohibitive costs. Conversely, confining updates to a low-dimensional subspace reduces variance but discards critical high-dimensional information, degrading performance. This creates a fundamental dilemma: full-space optimization is informative but has high variance, while subspace optimization has low variance but loses information. To break this trade-off, we propose ENGINE (Endogenous Variational MultiScale). Inspired by computational mechanics, we adapt static Variational MultiScale (VMS) theory to dynamic optimization, treating low- and high-dimensional spaces as resolved coarse and unresolved fine scales. Through a multigrid-style V-cycle, ENGINE alternates between spaces, dynamically probing fine-grained information in the high-dimensional space and injecting it to correct the efficient low-dimensional sampling process. Under appropriate assumptions, statistical analysis characterizes variance reduction and correction of curvature-induced bias, and convergence analysis establishes a stationarity bound for the corrected coarse updates. Evaluations on standard LLM benchmarks show that ENGINE achieves higher accuracy and faster convergence than existing ZO methods, achieving up to a $2.61\times$ speed-up in forward passes with zero additional peak memory overhead. Code is available at this https URL.

Title: End-to-End NTT on FPGA for FHE Acceleration

  • Authors: Mohamed Allam, Thomas Spendlhofer, Hugo Sanz-González, Antonio J. Peña
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.AR
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03325
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03325
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03325]] End-to-End NTT on FPGA for FHE Acceleration(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03325)
  • Keywords: secure, security, privacy
  • Abstract: Fully homomorphic encryption (FHE) enables privacy-preserving computation on encrypted data without decryption. Despite its strong security guarantees, FHE incurs substantial computational and memory overhead. In FHE-based deep learning inference, on average 63% of the CPU time is spent on the number theoretic transform (NTT) and its inverse (INTT), which reduce the complexity of polynomial multiplication from $O(N^2)$ to $O(N \log N)$. Prior FPGA works follow two approaches: accelerating entire FHE schemes, which yields significant speedups but ties them to specific parameters and algorithms, or accelerating only the NTT, which applies across schemes, algorithms, and libraries. Most FPGA NTT designs require interconnects that grow with parallelism and utilize significant resources, which complicates routing on multi-die FPGAs. Additionally, to the best of our knowledge, end-to-end NTT acceleration within an FHE context has so far been left unexplored. We design a fully unrolled, delay-commutator streaming NTT/INTT kernel, and floorplan it across the three dies of an AMD Alveo V80. At 400 MHz, the kernel transforms a polynomial of $2^{16}$ coefficients every 5.12 $\mu$s, the highest throughput among FPGA designs at this size. Offloaded end to end, it is up to 22x faster than the CPU per transform. We integrate the kernel into the Lattigo FHE library and evaluate it end to end. With 128-bit secure parameters, it accelerates core FHE operations, such as key switching and bootstrapping, by 1.8-2.2x, and neural network inference by 1.8-2.1x.

  • Abstract: Attention remains a substantial computational cost in Transformer training, particularly at long context lengths. We present FLAMIA, a family of attention kernels for Blackwell with MXFP8 and mixed NVFP4/FP8 training paths. Title: Characterizing When Language Models Take Harmful Actions to Protect Their Assigned Goals

  • Authors: Brandon Jaipersaud, Elliot Creager
  • Subjects: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03374
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03374
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03374]] Characterizing When Language Models Take Harmful Actions to Protect Their Assigned Goals(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03374)
  • Keywords: protect
  • Abstract: Recent language model (LM) safety failures have largely been studied as disparate phenomena. In agentic misalignment, a model blackmails an executive to avoid being replaced. In alignment faking, it complies with harmful requests during training to keep its values from being modified. We argue that these behaviours share a common decision structure: a model assigned a goal must either take a harmful action to preserve it or refuse and lose it. We formalize this as a goal-harm conflict and present the same conflict under various elicitation protocols that range from compact forced-choice prompts to simulated agentic environments. These protocols span two perspectives on LM decision-making, which we term (i) the logit view, reading preferences from answer-token probabilities, and (ii) the sampling view, classifying open-ended responses with an LM judge. Under the logit view, we show that goal importance and harm severity additively shape how a model resolves these value conflicts. Under the sampling view, we derive two factorizations of the harmful-action probability that safety evaluations commonly report. The first measures how well a preference read from answer tokens carries over into behaviour, such as making a harmful tool call. The second attributes a low harmful-action rate either to refusal or to responses that reach no resolution. We then show how fictional context changes a model's preference under the logit view, and how tool use and partial observability -- two features of agentic evaluations -- change its harmful-action rate under the sampling view. Our work offers a descriptive framework for studying how LMs resolve such value conflicts and for estimating low-probability harmful actions at a finer granularity than the aggregate harm rates commonly reported in safety evaluations.

Title: O2N2: Efficient Doubly Oblivious Approximate Nearest Neighbor Search

  • Authors: Zhechen Li, Zimai Guo, Mingxun Zhou, Chang Shi, Cheng Hong
  • Subjects: cs.CR, cs.DB
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03415
  • Pdf URL: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.03415
  • Copy Paste: [[2611.03415]] O2N2: Efficient Doubly Oblivious Approximate Nearest Neighbor Search(https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03415)
  • Keywords: security, privacy
  • Abstract: Privacy-preserving approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) search allows clients to search from an untrusted server without revealing sensitive queries, and is useful in scenarios like medical diagnostics and credit scoring. Among the various privacy-preserving primitives, trusted execution environments (TEEs) have much lower overhead than heavy cryptographic ones, yet they leak memory access patterns, enabling adversaries to infer query contents. Oblivious RAM (ORAM) can be used to hide those access patterns at $\mathrm{O}(\log N)$ asymptotic cost, but existing high-performance ANN search algorithms (e.g., IVF or HNSW) exhibit complex, data-dependent access patterns that are unfriendly for ORAM. Furthermore, standard ORAM constructions typically assume there exists a small amount of trusted storage, which weakens the security guarantees and is not always feasible. Solutions that eliminate such trusted storage (known as {\em double obliviousness}) typically degrade performance further to $\mathrm{O}(\log^2 N)$. We introduce O2N2, a doubly oblivious ANN framework built around single-path search in randomly rotated $k$-d tree forests. A pointer-based Circuit ORAM stores the search structure, with separate storage for compact internal nodes and vector-bearing leaves. Each visited leaf is processed by an oblivious selection network, and fixed-schedule duplicate removal and selection combine the local results into the final top-$k$. We evaluate O2N2 on real-world datasets and further test its scalability on 100 million vectors. For 10-NN and 100-NN search at the specified high-recall targets, it achieves a $9.8$--$125.9\times$ speedup over the fastest doubly oblivious baselines.

Title: Online Class Posteriors for Interpretable and Controllable Discrete Diffusion

  • Subjects: cs.LG
  • Abstract URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03454

  • Keywords: diffusion
  • Abstract: Discrete diffusion models remain far less understood than their continuous counterparts, despite recent breakthroughs across modalities, most notably in text. Leveraging tools from continuous diffusion, we formulate a theoretical framework to track the class-conditional posterior in an online manner by combining conditional and unconditional model predictions. In masked language modeling, this posterior offers highly interpretable dynamics: tokens aligned with a class boost the posterior, while contradictory ones suppress it.
